===PAGE 120=== (ocr, 48 lines)
   1|                                                                       Turkey Hill Elementary School
   2|                                                                                        2020-2021
   3|              The challenges of 2020 were monumental across the world, COVID-19 an unprecedented calamity with
   4|              profound effects for the world's population. No school in the United States was untouched. The pandemic
   5|              brought with it sweeping changes, some positive, others difficult, but our school community, like our
   6|               country, will never be quite the same.
   7|              Turkey Hill Elementary School is in its fourth year as a grade 3-5 building. There are currently 344
   8|              students, with 111 third graders, 108 fourth graders, and 125 fifth graders. We employ 25 teachers, 100%
   9|              of whom are licensed in their assignment area. Our special areas include physical education, art, music,
  10|              technology/library and social emotional learning. We are fortunate to have a half time assistant principal, a
  11|              full time nurse, a psychologist, BCBA, and a part time adjustment counselor/social worker. Other support
  12|              personnel who work across the district include two EL teachers, a speech and language pathologist, OT, and
  13|              PT.
  14|              January and February of 2020 were unremarkable: the annual TIGER anti-bullying presentation for grades
  15|              3 and 4, professional development in the Calkins Units for reading teachers, EMOTE pilot training (a
  16|              program that tracks student behavior), formative assessments for teacher evaluation, TST (teacher support
  17|              team) meetings and learning walks for administrators. The calm before the storm.
  18|              On a Friday afternoon in mid-March we were called together in an emergency meeting to grapple with
  19|              news about COVID-19. After hours of discussion and searching for the best information at hand, we made
  20|              the decision to close the schools for two weeks. It was a difficult call, but we understood we were working
  21|              in the best interests of our community to keep kids safe. That started us down a road to fully remote
  22|              learning right through June, a reality no one anticipated. Our teachers had a single day, the following
  23|              Monday, to gather their materials, laptops, teacher's guides, plan books, whatever they felt necessary. In a
  24|              remarkably short amount of time they created Google Classrooms and invited their students to it. We were
  25|              hampered by the fact that not every student had equitable access to computers and internet capacity. We
  26|              followed the state guidelines to provide enrichment work; we encouraged students to participate and learn,
  27|              but in the absence of accountability or grades it was an uphill battle. MCAS testing was canceled, as was
  28|              the 5th grade graduation ceremony, the field trips, assemblies, and end-of-year field days. Teachers were
  29|               only allowed to retrieve other items from school via appointments. In an attempt at some semblance of
  30|              school community, the administration created morning messages with birthday shout outs, question-of-the-
  31|              day challenges, as well as kid-humor riddles. Teachers were not obliged to "live meet" with students but
  32|              many did to reassure, answer questions, and provide support. They also worked closely with colleagues to
  33|              present a grade level set of instructions and expectations for consistency. We embraced the challenges as
  34|              they presented, and made it through the year amidst community fear, isolation, huge changes in working
  35|              conditions, childcare issues, and grieving the loss of "live" interactions with students.
  36|              Over the summer, the superintendent created a number of committees to plan for fall reopening. We talked
  37|              through all the scenarios: fully remote, fully in person, hybrid. Ultimately, given the constraints of social
  38|              distancing we settled on a hybrid model with a fully remote option. That meant we would have no more
  39|              than half of our students in two days a week, either Tuesday and Thursday or Wednesday and Friday, and
  40|              Monday became a fully remote day for all students/staff so teachers would have time to collaborate and
  41|              plan in this new setting. About 100 students (of about 350) opted for fully remote classes. One teacher
  42|              from each grade level took on that responsibility. The other 250 students were divided into Cohort A or B.
  43|              Classrooms were reconfigured to hold 12-13 desks, spread six feet apart. Teachers created buffer zones of
  44|              six feet at the front of each room to use their white boards. Hand sanitizer was distributed throughout the
  45|              building, including dispensers at each entrance, and students were obliged to use it upon arrival. Signage
  46|              was posted that gave students safe places outside the classroom to take a water/mask break, to stay to the
  47|              right side of the hallway, to line up six feet apart.
  48|                                                                                              120

===PAGE 121=== (ocr, 46 lines)
   1|              It was understood that all students and staff would wear masks in classrooms, and mask breaks each hour
   2|              were added into schedules. Routes were planned for entering and exiting the building, as well as
   3|              transitioning to lunch, special areas, and breaks. Outside areas were identified for each cohort and grade
   4|              where students could safely play. The playground was off limits because of safety concerns. The
   5|              playground equipment (balls and jump ropes) were put away. Students could bring their own soccer balls
   6|              but had to commit to hands-free play. Teachers had to reconsider when/how to distribute or collect papers;
   7|              plastic gloves were distributed to each room to allow children access to classroom libraries. Team teachers
   8|              and special area teachers sprayed down every desk at every transition.
   9|              Individual desk shields were purchased and distributed for small or 1:1 group work. Chromebooks were
  10|              purchased and distributed at the beginning of the year so that every student had equitable access, and if
  11|              there were home challenges with the internet, equipment was sent home to create "hot spots". Boxes of
  12|              disposable masks and other PPE was distributed, and professional development was provided. Extra
  13|              headphones were distributed with "safe" plastic covers for the earpieces.
  14|              The first ten days of school were dedicated to professional development opportunities for teachers. There
  15|              were new programs to use, strategies to implement, and obstacles to overcome. They planned how to
  16|              present to A cohort students while still providing instruction for remote B cohort students. They learned
  17|              new programs. They prepared for a new normal.
  18|              The lunchroom was transformed. Instead of 12 to a table, there are either 2 or 3 to each table, all facing
  19|              forward, designated and assigned spots, six feet apart. There is no longer a table for condiments or utensils.
  20|              Every item is in disposable containers and custodians walk around with rolling containers for trash.
  21|              Students remain seated unless there is a specific reason. At first glance it was difficult to fathom, that
  22|              lunchrooms which are meant for informal social interaction as much as eating lunch had become such a
  23|              sterile environment. Remarkably, in the cafeteria and throughout their days, students have adapted and
  24|              cooperated with the new rigid safety protocols. They are just happy to see each other, and overall
  25|              enthusiasm about coming to school has never been so universally apparent.
  26|              We developed a new arrival and dismissal protocol for car students: in the morning, they entered through a
  27|              separate entrance as bus riders and in the afternoon they spread out in the cafeteria and waited for their
  28|              names to be called as their parents pulled in to the driveway. Initially that was a slow moving process, but
  29|              eventually parents faithfully displayed their rearview mirror name tags for easy identification. Buses were
  30|              exited two at a time to limit any clustering. Again, all students were prompted to use hand sanitizer when
  31|              entering the building.
  32|              School visitors were infrequent. IEP meetings were held via Zoom, as were first term parent conferences.
  33|              Faculty meetings and building leadership team meetings were held via computer. Administrative meetings
  34|              were also remote. Individual conferences between admin and teachers, grade level meetings, and vertical
  35|              team meetings were held online. The front office door was locked, a startling transformation from a
  36|              frequent staff check in to a need-based only visit. Strict protocols were implemented for anyone at the front
  37|              door, including "can I help you?" before the door was opened.
  38|              In December we were excited to receive the necessary means to reinvent the hybrid model. Every teacher
  39|              received a large monitor to interact with remote students, a microphone/speaker that allowed remote
  40|              students to hear both the teacher and the other students, a docking station, and a tablet that allows the
  41|              teacher to "write" on the presentation screen. They started to prepare to move to a fully live stream model
  42|              for term three in late January of 2021.
  43|              Another significant change has been the introduction of Ready, an evidence based reading and math
  44|              diagnostic with learning platforms. All students participated in the initial assessment in October and it
  45|              provided valuable information as to the strengths and areas for improvement for each student.
  46|                                                                                              121

===PAGE 122=== (ocr, 32 lines)
   1|              We were able to purchase the reading platform, which is individualized to support every child's growth.
   2|              The ideal time spent is 45 minutes weekly and in November and December we acknowledged and
   3|              celebrated students who put in the effort to meet that expectations; the same occurred for math work in the
   4|              IXL platform. iReady offers a level of data detail we have previously not been able to access, right down to
   5|              students' mastery of individual standards. It is meant to inform instruction for teachers, allowing them to
   6|              focus on areas of concern, as well as providing challenging work for students who excel.
   7|              With the fortunate addition of a literacy specialist/Title 1 coordinator, we have been able to provide small
   8|              group instruction for student's at all three grade levels in both math and ELA. This is a hugely important
   9|              benefit to our school, to target specific areas for improvement and provide that service. Our coordinator has
  10|              hired on three tutors who consistently meet with kids on their off cohort days and work with them. She has
  11|              also provided several professional development opportunities for staff on iReady: how to analyze, interpret
  12|              and use the data it provides. We have also received extensive support from our interim director of teaching
  13|              and learning. She works closely with our literacy specialists for the Ready support and has organized
  14|              extensive pd on a long list of teaching tools, including Epic, Nearpod, Google classroom, Footsteps to
  15|              Brilliance, Newsela, Jamboard, Kahoot, and Padlet. She is a frequent visitor to classrooms to provide
  16|              individualized support for teachers. She organizes our vertical team meetings to document productivity.
  17|              She organizes the monthly feedback from staff, students, and parents to help administration analyze and
  18|              respond to the trends. Both professionals ensure that our curriculum is evidence based, research driven, and
  19|              aligned to our state standards. Our journey to improve literacy and math skills for all students have
  20|              wonderful new navigators.
  21|              It's been a steep learning curve for our staff and students this year to integrate the technology into learning,
  22|              but we acknowledge that it has forced us to move forward to a "connected" level that enhances educational
  23|              opportunities. We have all been swept into 215 century teaching and forever gone are the days of overhead
  24|              projectors, reams of worksheets and kids missing school unnecessarily. While we anticipate the hope of
  25|              being mask free, of offering a high five or even a hug without repercussion, of sitting close to friends or
  26|              playing on the slide and swing set again, we also embrace the 1:1 student to Chromebook capacity and all
  27|              that comes with that. We will all continue to use hand sanitizer consistently and maybe stay home
  28|              unhesitatingly when we're not feeling good. But, we can also connect to each other despite snow storms,
  29|              doctor's appointments, or having to quarantine. 2021 holds promise.
  30|              Respectfully Submitted,
  31|              Heidi Champagne, Principal
  32|                                                                                              122

===PAGE 124=== (ocr, 44 lines)
   1|                                                                           Lunenburg Middle School
   2|                                                                                    February 2021
   3|              Lunenburg Middle School provides a rigorous academic curriculum as well as strong programs that support
   4|              students' social and emotional growth. These programs ensure that each child becomes a self-motivated,
   5|              creative, lifelong learner in a safe, stimulating environment that encourages diversity and open honest
   6|              communication. The dedicated and accomplished staff of professionals within the school embraces the
   7|              philosophy of educating the whole child academically, socially, and emotionally. This philosophy is a
   8|              means by which all students become positive community members that possess the skills to be successful in
   9|              high school and beyond.
  10|               Lunenburg Middle School is in its fifth year as a co-located middle - high school serving grades six
  11|              through eight. Effective, student centered middle schools embrace interdisciplinary teams of teachers that
  12|              enable teachers to work with smaller groups of students. Smaller groups of students allow teachers to learn
  13|              and engage students in their preferred learning style, making them more confident and successful.
  14|              Our effective middle school model is student centered and provides opportunities to explore a variety of
  15|              different interests and talents through special areas and enrichment classes and extracurricular activities.
  16|              One of our goals is for students to discover something they are passionate about and want to continue
  17|              exploring as they move through high school and beyond. We will continue to seek out opportunities for
  18|              students that foster critical thinking and problem solving skills that prepare them for high school and
  19|              beyond or where their passion may lead them to.
  20|                                                                         Hybrid model of instruction
  21|              On August 5, 2020, due to the global pandemic, the Lunenburg School Committee voted and approved a
  22|              hybrid model of instruction for Lunenburg Public Schools for the beginning of the 2020-2021 school year.
  23|              The hybrid model includes limited, smaller groups of learners in the building at one time and some students
  24|              learning exclusively from home remotely.
  25|              Prior to the students return to school in September, we faced many logistical and educational challenges
  26|              with the hybrid model of instruction. Classrooms needed to be reorganized to allow students six feet of
  27|              distance between their peers, and their teachers. Disinfecting protocols and mask mandates were adopted.
  28|              The cafeteria was redesigned to ensure and how students were seated in accordance with CDC guidelines.
  29|              Additionally, the food distribution process needed to be changed.
  30|              Outside areas were designated for students to take "mask breaks" with their classroom teachers. Space
  31|              markers were placed in hallways and offices to assist students in keeping six feet apart. School buses also
  32|              needed to adhere to physical distancing guidelines therefore the number of students allowed to ride the bus
  33|              to school was greatly reduced.
  34|              Students who chose the hybrid model of instruction were divided into "cohorts" or smaller groups of
  35|              learners that attend in person classes twice a week. Assignments of students into cohorts took district wide
  36|              coordination as families with children in different schools needed their children attending school on the
  37|              same day in the same cohort.
  38|              The "Zoom" platform of teleconferencing became an essential communication tool. Parent Zoom Drop In
  39|              meetings were held to inform parents of the changes and guidelines of reopening school. Lunenburg Middle
  40|               School has held several Zoom meetings for parents.
  41|              With students learning both at home and in person, each student now required a Chrome book in order to
  42|              access learning. All students at LMS were assigned a Chrome book. LMS faculty became skilled at
  43|              utilizing educational platforms such as Google Classroom and Google Meets.
  44|                                                                                              124

===PAGE 125=== (ocr, 36 lines)
   1|              The district invested in a variety of on line tools and assessments to assist teachers to assess and educate
   2|              students both in person and remote. Before school opened in the fall, all teachers were provided with ten
   3|              days of professional development to become more proficient in utilizing the tools necessary for effective
   4|              hybrid learning.
   5|               The social and emotional health of our student population was our top priority when students returned to
   6|              school in September. LMS put several safety nets in place for students. Child study teams were developed
   7|              that met frequently to discuss and review any students who were struggling and referred them to our
   8|              behavioral health support team (BHST). The BHST reviewed students referred to them and offered supports
   9|              to both the student and their families. Both teams were responsible for tracking students' progress, or lack
  10|              of progress, to determine what strategies were successful or what other resources we could offer.
  11|              In order to support students, LMS also created the position of Engagement Coordinator for the 2020-2021
  12|              school year. The Engagement Coordinator assists students who struggle to attend classes regularly or are
  13|              not completing assignments to the best of their ability. The Engagement Coordinator works with students
  14|              and their families to identify roadblocks in the student's success and develops an engagement success plan
  15|              with helpful strategies for students and families.
  16|              In early December, Lunenburg Middle School 8"h grade teachers piloted "live streaming", a synchronous
  17|              learning model. Students were now able to engage in face-to-face instruction while at home on remote
  18|              learning days. Live streaming increased the amount of face time students have with their teachers and
  19|              assisted with engaging students who were at home with quality instruction. On January 29, Lunenburg
  20|              Middle School adopted synchronous learning at all grade levels to adhere to the new requirements of the
  21|              Department of Elementary and Secondary Education of 35 hours of synchronous instruction over a ten day
  22|              period.
  23|              Although the pandemic has forced us to postpone most after school activities, faculty at Lunenburg Middle
  24|              School worked to offer students virtual access to some after school activities. Yearbook Club and LMS
  25|              Science Olympiad were able to meet virtually and our Ski Club was still able to participate, without the bus
  26|              transportation. Some seventh and eighth graders were able to participate in high school athletics like Cross
  27|              Country and a pandemic version of Indoor Track, held outdoors.
  28|              Lunenburg Middle School continues to provide high quality educational services and supports that ensure
  29|              student success and promote a passion for lifelong learning even during this unprecedented time of a global
  30|              pandemic. The key components to our success continue to be our talented faculty committed to making a
  31|              positive impact on students, our parents and community who support our teaching and learning, and most of
  32|              all, our students who inspire us through their commitment to learning during this difficult time. Together
  33|              we continue to make the difference in the lives of students.
  34|              Respectfully Submitted,
  35|              Tim Santry, Principal
  36|                                                                                              125

===PAGE 126=== (ocr, 44 lines)
   1|                                                                            Lunenburg High School
   2|                                                                                       2020-2021
   3|             Lunenburg High School has been busy with academic and school activities in 2020-2021. This year has
   4|              been a rollercoaster with all the changes and protocols due to the COVID-19 pandemic. Teachers have
   5|             provided our students with rigorous coursework and hold our students to high standards. Teachers have
   6|             worked so hard to completely change how they deliver curriculum during this pandemic. Our students have
   7|             met these challenges through collaboration, self-advocacy, and a strong work ethic. All students in grades 9
   8|              -12 have been issued a Chromebook and there are wireless capabilities throughout the building. All
   9|             teachers are using Google Classroom, numerous online teaching platforms, the interactive projectors, and
  10|             voice-lift capabilities. Our students are fortunate to be in a building that can meet the needs of the 21st
  11|              century learner.
  12|              During the fall sports season, our students practiced and competed on our fields and artificial turf field.
  13|              During the fall season, most of our fields were being used from 3:00 pm to 8:00 pm.
  14|              The winter season has been very busy, as well. The gym for a large portion of the afternoon and evening is
  15|             being used by our basketball teams. Additionally, we have the hockey team practicing and competing at:
  16|             Wallace Civic Center.
  17|              NEASC ACCREDITATION
  18|              Lunenburg High School has been accredited after going through the NEASC evaluation process.
  19|              Lunenburg High School is currently working on growth areas and incorporating the graduate profile for the
  20|             next NEASC accreditation process.
  21|             SCHOOL/COMMUNITY PARTNERSHIPS
  22|              Throughout the year, Lunenburg High School students are involved in a multitude of fundraising and
  23|             collection endeavors. Lunenburg High School participated in a canned goods drive to help feed people in
  24|              need. Around the holiday season, advisories, LEA, STUCO, NHS and numerous other agencies collected
  25|             items for the Annual Lunenburg Public Schools Secretaries Gift Card Drive. The LEA made a significant
  26|              contribution to the gift card drive to help families in need. Thank you to all who participated in these very
  27|              worthy causes.
  28|              MCAS RESULTS
  29|              We do not have MCAS results this year because MCAS was canceled, by the DESE, due to COVID-19 and
  30|             the closure.
  31|              COMMUNITY BUILDING
  32|              National Honor Society successfully supported the "Gift Card Drive" during the holidays, helping to
  33|              provide joy to needy children and families in conjunction with local agencies. The World Hunger Task
  34|              Force will be raising money through Penny Wars this spring. The money will be donated toward the fight
  35|             against hunger.
  36|              CURRICULUM
  37|              The staff at Lunenburg High School has been outstanding at working through this pandemic. Having to
  38|              completely change how we educate students. The work they have put in and the dedication to the student
  39|             body is commendable. The staff have focused professional development in 2020-2021 working on social &
  40|              emotional health, online learning, vertical teaming, rubrics, the graduate profile, and technology. A large
  41|             focus of professional development has been on online learning and how to teach students during a global
  42|              pandemic.
  43|              Respectfully Submitted, Rob McGrath, Principal
  44|                                                                                              126

===PAGE 130=== (ocr, 53 lines)
   1|               As I reflected on the 2019-2020 school year, and considered what might be included in the 2020 Annual
   2|               Report, I was reminded of the progress and accomplishments made by our students and staff, during an
   3|               incredibly challenging time. While each year at Monty Tech is exciting and includes many "firsts",
   4|                traditional instruction ended abruptly in 2019-2020, and students and instructors were forced to turn to
   5|               technology to watch, learn and collaborate. The all-important hands-on learning was not possible because
   6|               of the unexpected pandemic, and so for the first time ever, our vocational instructors relied solely on their
   7|               creativity, their understanding of instructional technology, and their technical skills to carry them through
   8|               the end of the year. Our academic teachers collaborated to create lessons and assessments that would
   9|               effectively measure learning in a remote setting, and explored countless apps and platforms to increase
  10|               engagement. Students who looked forward to events like SkillsUSA competitions, prom and graduation,
  11|               grappled with disappointment and the unknown, and Guidance Counselors worked diligently to reach out to
  12|               students who were now more isolated from their peers than ever before. To say 2019-2020 was challenging
  13|               may be an understatement, but to disregard our progress as an educational community would be a disservice
  14|               to everyone who worked so hard to serve our students. While the traditional school year may have been
  15|               abbreviated, so many incredible achievements were recognized in this historic year.
  16|               A project more than 7 years in the making, the Monty Tech Veterinary Clinic, opened and began providing
  17|               affordable veterinary care to pets in need, across our district. Routine wellness care visits, affordable
  18|               medications and vaccinations, diagnostic services, primary care surgical services, stable urgent care,
  19|               compassionate end of life care, and dentistry services were delivered by our talented Veterinary Medical
  20|               Director, Dr. Kayla Sample. Students worked in the new state-of-the-art clinic, learning techniques and
  21|               gaining knowledge that align with veterinary assistant programs across the Commonwealth. In fact, Becker
  22|               College has recognized the Monty Tech Veterinary Science program for its rigor and content, and has
  23|               offered qualified program graduates up to 13 college credits. Further, relying on the school's trusted model
  24|               of school-to-business partnerships, a unique collaboration between Monty Tech and VCA has resulted in co-
  25|               op placements for Seniors in the program.
  26|               In August 2019, Monty Tech accepted a $384,257 award from the Massachusetts Skills Cabinet. This
  27|               award provided funding to update technology and equipment in two shops: Health Occupations and
  28|               Automotive Technology. Health Occupations students worked and learned in updated shop facilities, to
  29|               include new hospital beds and lifts, as well as new diagnostic equipment to train students in the proper
  30|               procedures for testing for cholesterol, blood sugar, lead, lung volume, and more. In addition, the program
  31|               received state-of-the-art virtual dissection technology, bringing lessons in anatomy and physiology to new
  32|               heights. This equipment, provided by Anatomage and most often found on college campuses, immediately
  33|               engaged students, bringing a deeper level of knowledge and understanding to students as they explored the
  34|               human body and its complex systems.
  35|               Students in the Automotive Technology program were introduced to an all-new simulation lab, that now
  36|               includes the latest technology in trainers and simulators aligned with NATEF standards. The shop was
  37|               updated to include new automotive lifts, floor jacks, engine stands, chargers, an air table, and a redesigned
  38|               tool crib with all new hand tools. Further, in 2019-2020, in partnership with Snap On Tools, instructors
  39|               rolled out a more sophisticated technical training program and increased opportunities to earn all-new
  40|               diagnostic credentials, validating their experience and ability to diagnose and service today's computer-
  41|               controlled vehicles.          With new curriculum, technology and the addition of NC3 Automotive Diagnostic
  42|               Certifications to the program, our Automotive Technology program graduates will enter a competitive
  43|               workforce poised for success.
  44|               Students who attend Monty Tech have chosen a high school experience unlike any other. While completing
  45|               all of the same high school requirements of students enrolled in a traditional, comprehensive high school,
  46|               our students are also learning a valuable trade - skills they will carry with them for a lifetime. In the most
  47|               challenging times, our students are called upon to demonstrate creativity, innovation and technical skill
  48|               proficiency, and though face-to-face instruction may have been interrupted, their achievements continued.
  49|               On behalf of our talented educators and administrators, who remain focused on delivering the highest
  50|               quality academic and vocational-technical education possible, I am delighted to present the District's 2019-
  51|               2020 annual report to you.
  52|               Respectfully submitted, Sheila M. Harrity, Ed.D., Superintendent-Director
  53|                                                                                              130

   1|             Our Mission
   2|             Every student will graduate from Montachusett Regional Vocational Technical School with the skills,
   3|             knowledge, and abilities to be a productive and effective member of an ever-changing society.
   4|             Our District
   5|             Montachusett Regional Vocational Technical School is a four-year career and technical high school serving
   6|             the member towns of:
   7|               Ashburnham                                              Harvard                                                Princeton
   8|               Ashby                                                   Holden                                                 Royalston
   9|               Athol                                                  Hubbardston                                             Sterling
  10|               Barre                                                   Lunenburg                                              Templeton
  11|               Fitchburg                                               Petersham                                              Westminster
  12|               Gardner                                                 Phillipston                                            Winchendon
  13|               Leadership
  14|               The leadership team at Montachusett Regional Vocational Technical School is comprised of ten
  15|               talented administrators whose varied educational backgrounds, professional experiences, and areas of
  16|               expertise contribute to the success of the school. Working collaboratively, and under the direction of
  17|               the Superintendent and Principal, the team has transformed the school into one of the most sought-
  18|               after high schools in North Central Massachusetts.

  29|                Enrollment
  30|             On June 1, 2020, student enrollment at Monty Tech included 1,408 students in grades nine through
  31|             twelve, representing each of the district's eighteen sending communities. Each class of students is
  32|             comprised of a relatively equal balance of male vs. female students, and each student attending Monty
  33|              Tech has elected to do so, prepared to explore an interest in one of the school's twenty-one rigorous
  34|             vocational-technical programs. While the school currently offers only five programs non-traditional
  35|             for male students, and twelve programs non-traditional for female students, we are proud to have a
  36|             student population that is well- balanced by gender and a variety of academic interests and
  37|             achievements.

===PAGE 133=== (ocr, 46 lines)
   1|               Approximately 31% of the graduating class of 2020 reported plans to enroll at a 4-year college/university
   2|               upon graduation. By earning college credits while still in high school, these students will save both
   3|               time and money as they pursue advanced educational programs.
   4|               Financial Report
   5|               In an effort to develop a cost-effective budget for the fiscal year 2019-2020, a great deal of effort was put
   6|               forth by the School Committee, administration, and staff. The final fiscal year 2019-2020 Educational Plan
   7|               totaled $28,760,202 which represents a 3.62% increase over the 2018-2019 Educational Plan. The District's
   8|               FY20 budget only exceeds the minimum spending required by Massachusetts General Law Chapter 70 by
   9|               $145,657 or .6%.
  10|                The District was audited in December 2020 as part of the yearly financial audit by the accounting firm of
  11|               Melanson, Heath and Co. from Greenfield, MA and expects to receive a good report.
  12|               Grants and Contracts
  13|               Monty Tech continues to pursue grant funding on an annual basıs. These tunds help provide many
  14|               educational and social services to the student population. For fiscal year 2020, state and federal grant
  15|               sources provided the school with $978,827. Programs funded by these grants include: Essential Health
  16|               Services, Improving Teacher Quality, Special Education Services and Program Improvement, Title I
  17|               Support, Perkins Occupational Education, Student Support and Academic Enrichment and Marine Corp
  18|               Junior Reserve Officer Training.
  19|               Academic Achievement
  20|               During the spring of 2020, the administration of the MCAS was postponed due to COVID-19 safety
  21|               precautions. Last year, Monty Tech's passing rate on the English Language Arts was 99%, Mathematics
  22|               97%, Biology 99%. Monty Tech students exceeded the state averages in all 3 subjects. With regard to our
  23|               Accountability status, Monty Tech made "Substantial Progress Toward Meeting Targets."
  24|                Monty Tech is committed to providing students with rigorous STEM learning opportunities, and so in
  25|               recent years, the curriculum has been expanded to include Advanced Placement Programs (two English
  26|               courses, two mathematics courses, two science courses, and one computer science course). In addition,
  27|               teachers have participated in meaningful, high-quality professional development which has enabled us to
  28|               successfully implement two Project Lead the Way biomedical courses and 2 dual enrollment science courses
  29|               (Biology and Biotechnology).
  30|               During the spring of 2020, students at Montachusett Regional Vocational Technical School continued to
  31|               demonstrate high academic achievement, earning commendable scores on AP exams. It is important to
  32|               remember that these exams took place during remote learning. AP exams were modified significantly prior
  33|               to testing, the testing environment was remote, and teachers may have faced some challenges in planning
  34|               some of the components of the curriculum (due to COVID-19). Although we are pleased with the results,
  35|               we are aware that these issues, in addition to potential internal scaling applied by the College Board, may
  36|               have significantly impacted the final results.

  23|              Vocational Projects in the District Communities
  24|              Unlike students in traditional, comprehensive high schools, students at Monty Tech are asked to put their
  25|              education into practice on a daily basis. Students across the twenty-one different vocational-technical
  26|              education programs are building homes, reconstructing damaged properties, repairing service vehicles,
  27|              making walkways more accessible, and performing countless community services. Unfortunately, the
  28|              widespread pandemic forced a school closure, and vocational instruction was delivered remotely beginning
  29|              March 2020. In turn, opportunities for hands-on learning were impacted, and cooperative education
  30|              placements available to students were also restricted, as business partners                                            were unable to sustain full
  31|              staffing.
  32|               Auto Body Collision Repair Technology: The Monty Tech Auto Body program benefitted from much-
  33|              needed, new instructional equipment in 2019-2020, receiving a new Snap-On Air conditioning machine and
  34|              a wall-mounted pressure washer, which provided additional floor space. Students were trained with the Pro-
  35|              Spot M.I.G brazing and aluminum M.I.G.Welder, and all Seniors received their EPA 6 H Certificate, I-Car
  36|              Pro Level 1 in Refinishing and Non- Structural Repair Certifications. A bridge project was completed in
  37|              collaboration with Worcester Polytechnic Institute (WPI), and soon thereafter our community service
  38|              projects were discontinued due to the pandemic. Three Seniors and two Juniors earned co-op placements,
  39|              and students performed quite well at the SkillsUSA District competition, earning a gold medal in Collision
  40|              Damage Appraisal, a silver medal in Refinishing, and silver and bronze medals for Collision Repair. (Total
  41|              student enrollment: 63)

===PAGE 135=== (ocr, 55 lines)
   1|             Automotive Technology: The Monty Tech Automotive Technology program was awarded the FY20 Skills
   2|             Capital Grant, and as a result students and instructors began the 2019-2020 school year in a shop that
   3|             included a newly designed and outfitted tool crib, five new floor jacks, three new automotive lifts, one air
   4|             table, five engine stands, three chargers, and an all-new Auto Lab that includes high tech trainers and
   5|              simulators alıgned with NATEF standards. Two additional certification opportunities (digital multımeters
   6|              and scan tool applications) were made available to students, using the newly purchased Snap-On NC3
   7|             certified training equipment. These industry-recognized credentials and the new equipment will enhance our
   8|             students' level of competency within the automotive industry for years to come. Until the pandemic forced a
   9|             bulding closure, students and staff in the Automotive Technology program had worked on or diagnosed
  10|             more than 200 vehicles.                     Students performed repairs that included transmission replacements, intake
  11|             manifolds, brake work, and various other technical repairs, demonstrating technical skills that are required for
  12|             any service technician in our area. Three Seniors and one Junior earned co-op placements, while instructors
  13|             continued to utilize e-learning program options to keep the remaining students on track and engaged during
  14|             the closure. (Total student enrollment: 62)
  15|             Business Technology: Monty Tech's Business Technology program continues to benefit from a unique
  16|             partnership with Workers Credit Union. The opportunity to participate in teller training and financial literacy
  17|             workshops has been met with great enthusiasm from students, who completed 9 modules and earned a
  18|             financial literacy certificate. Instructors, in collaboration with their post-secondary colleagues, continue to
  19|             refine and develop new articulation agreements. The agreement with Mount Wachusett Community College
  20|             will provide qualified students with college credits at no cost to the student. Students are eligible to receive
  21|             24 credits from Mount Wachusett Community College. Opportunities to demonstrate customer service, cash
  22|             handling, and accounting skills are ever-present as the Business Technology students successfully operate an
  23|             in-house retail location, The Gear House School Store, and the Monty Tech Greenhouse. Five Seniors and
  24|             five Juniors were out on co-op placements during the 2019-2020 school year. (Total student enrollment: 71)
  25|             Cabinetmaking: Monty Tech Cabinetmaking students are called upon for a number of projects small and
  26|             large, across the campus and beyond. 2019-2020 was no exception. Students completed projects that
  27|             include: bulding a beautiful solid oak cabinet for the Fitchburg Alumni class of 1969, to be presented as a
  28|             gift to Fitchburg High School's library; designing and using CNC technology to manufacture signage for the
  29|             Devens Fire Department; and collaborating with Worcester Polytechnic Institute to mill and CNC over 400
  30|             pieces of ash to be used for strength testing for their engineering students. Sophomores in the Cabinetmaking
  31|             program also designed and constructed three hundred fifty 10'x19" sapele charcuterie boards with pocketed
  32|             cut-outs to accommodate two serving dishes and slate tray. The finished pieces will be used to raise funds for
  33|             the Student Scholarship Program, supporting the Class of 2021. Eight Seniors and four Juniors earned co-op
  34|             placements, representing the program so well. (Total student enrollment: 67)
  35|             CAD/Drafting & Design: In 2019-2020, the Monty Tech CAD/Drafting & Design program welcomed a new
  36|             instructor, Jesse Veinotte, whose talents will help guide the Freshmen and Senior experience for years to
  37|             come. While the school closure certainly curtailed the number of community serve jobs our students were
  38|             able to complete, the curriculum was easily adapted into an online platform, and students were able to
  39|             achieve a number of valuable industry-recognized certifications, including AutoCAD, Inventor, Revit, and
  40|             Solidworks. Twelve students advanced to the SkillsUSA States competition, representing the program well,
  41|             while two Senior students were proud to earn coveted co-op placements, working at Process Cooling in
  42|             Leominster, MA and United Plastic Fabricating in North Andover, MA. (Total student enrollment: 61)
  43|             Cosmetology: The Monty Tech Cosmetology program is one of the only programs that students may leave
  44|             and directly enter the industry, fully-licensed and ready for a career. The state-approved program is rigorous
  45|             and provides students with countless opportunities to refine their cosmetology skills on clientele who
  46|             understand and appreciate the learning environment. 2019-2020 was an exceptionally busy year for the
  47|             Monty Tech Cosmetology students, who were compelled to raise funds for a local family in need, and did so
  48|             by hosting "Staff Nights." Monty Tech staff and their families were invited into the shop for discounted
  49|             services, and proceeds were donated to support a young girl who tragically lost her father in an acident.
  50|             Their dedication to community service and "giving back" was a hallmark of the Cosmetology Class of 2020,
  51|              and a testament to the students who worked so hard and learned so much while in the Monty Tech program.
  52|             Eighteen students took and passed the Cosmetology State Board exam, and are now prepared for careers in vetodey bary sulened
  53|             the field. In addition, a total of one nine Seniors earned co-op placements in local salons - more than any
  54|             year before. (Total student enrollment: 86)

   1|             Culinary Arts: In a typical year, Monty Tech Culinary Arts students and instructors operate a full-service
   2|             restaurant and bakery, serving 90-120 patrons daily.                                They might also showcase their talents at regional
   3|             events and fundraisers, working side-by-side with some of the area's finest chefs. But 2019-2020 was no
   4|             ordinary year. The school's Mountain Room Restaurant closed in March, and all in-person dining/serving
   5|             experiences were cancelled for the students. Curriculum transitioned from in-person to a virtual platform,
   6|             which is challenging for the culinary trade, but the instructors did so effectively, seeking meaningful
   7|             opportunities for their students to continue to develop in their chosen trade.                                             Despite the pandemic, all
   8|             students successfully earned Allergen Awareness and ServSafe Certifications, which are important credentials
   9|             to have for any entry-level position in the culinary and hospitality trades. (Total student enrollment: 97)
  10|             Dental Assisting: A critical component of the Monty Tech Dental Assisting curriculum is the preparation for
  11|             the Dental Assisting National Board certification exams. In 2019-2020, all students who sat for both the
  12|             DANB Infection Control exam and the DANB Radiology exam, passed. This 100% pass rate is a true
  13|             testament to the high quality instruction in the Monty Tech Dental Assisting program, and something we
  14|             continue to be so proud of. A total of four students (two Seniors and two Juniors) earned co-op placements,
  15|             while the remaining Juniors and Seniors participated in affiliation/externship experiences in area dental
  16|             clinics and offices. Monty Tech Dental Assisting students and instructors also worked closely with local
  17|             health provider, Community Health Connections, and provided dental services to more than 35 students in
  18|             need through this beneficial school-based dental hygiene program. This experience provided valuable hands-
  19|             on experience, as students were called upon to assist the staff from CHC during each dental procedure
  20|             performed. (Total student enrollment: 60)
  21|             Early Childhood Education: Students and instructors in the Monty Tech Early Childhood Education program
  22|             are committed to community service activities, and the 2019-2020 school year was no exception. Students
  23|             and instructors raised funds for SkillsUSA Change for Children and held a Sesame Street themed "Baby
  24|             Show" for the Gardner VNA "Healthy Families" program. Two Juniors and one Senior were out on co-op,
  25|             gaining valuable experience working with young children, and instructors worked closely with colleagues at
  26|             Fitchburg State University to develop the program's first-ever articulation agreement between the two
  27|             institutions. Fitchburg State University will now grant qualified program graduates up to 6 college credits, at
  28|             no charge, recognizing the work they have completed while enrolled in the Monty Tech Early Childhood
  29|             Education Program. The Monty Tech Child Care Center continues to operate at full capacity but in a hybrid
  30|             model, providing meaningful hands-on learning opportunities for all students in the Early Childhood
  31|             Education program. (Total student enrollment: 60)
  32|             Electrical: Throughout 2019-2020, students and instructors in the Electrical program wired numerous
  33|             machines, equipment, computers, and lights throughout Monty Tech. Students and instructors successfully
  34|             completed wiring new equipment in the AutoBody Shop, and CAD/ Drafting & Design shop. The students
  35|             wired the new electronic parking lot gate, and began wiring two homes in Ashburnham, constructed in
  36|             collaboration with Habitat for Humanity. Unfortunately, when the school closed in March 2020, all hands-on
  37|             projects for students ceased, so wiring those homes was completed by our instructors, who volunteered their
  38|             time to do so. Fourteen Seniors and two Juniors earned co-op placements, and continued their work
  39|             throughout the Spring and into the summer months. The Freshman class was filled with first choice students,
  40|             which is a credit to the talents of the program's newest instructor, Alex Thibeault. (Total student enrollment:
  41|             Engineering Technology: The Engineering Technology program at Monty Tech is a rigorous program,
  42|             infused with a nationally certified curriculum provided by Project Lead the Way, and countless hands-on
  43|             learning opportunities that provide students with opportunities to demonstrate creativity, make connections
  44|             with prior learning, and earn college credits. On October 22, 2019, the school welcomed Lt. Governor Karyn
  45|             Polito, to celebrate the completion and ribbon cutting of the school's new ARM (Automation, Robotics and
  46|             Mechatronics) Lab. All Engineering Technology students benefitted from the new technology available in
  47|             the lab, including three Amatrol tabletop mechatronics learning systems with Pegasus robots, one Fanuc LR
  48|             Mate 200ID/4S robot, one Festo MPS 203 mechatronics system, and one Stratasys F270 3D printer. All
  49|             Freshmen completed the OSHA 10-hour General Industry training and certification program, while two
  50|             Seniors participated in meaningful co-op placements. (Total student enrollment: 57)

===PAGE 137=== (ocr, 52 lines)
   1|               Graphic Communications: Throughout the 2019-2020 school year, the students in Monty Tech's Graphic
   2|               Communications shop produced numerous projects for our district cities and towns, and a variety of non-
   3|               profit organizations in the district. Due to the COVID-19 pandemic, hands-on learning opportunities were
   4|               halted in March, so students were unable to complete all community services that were requested. A
   5|               program highlight for the year was having twelve students earn awards at the Printing Industries of New
   6|               England's Award of Excellence Gala. Having their talents recognized by industry professionals is not only
   7|               rewarding to the student, but also validates the efforts of our talented teaching staff. Four Seniors and one
   8|               Junior pursued and earned co-op placements. Of the twenty-one talented Seniors who graduated from the
   9|               Graphic Communication program in 2020, all twenty-one were accepted into colleges/universities and eight
  10|               chose to enter the workforce in related careers. (Total student enrollment: 85)
  11|               Health Occupations: The Health Occupations program continues to be one of the more popular programs
  12|               here at Monty Tech, providing a rigorous education grounded in current medical knowledge and practice. A
  13|               Skills Capital Grant award provided the program with $180,246 in additional funding, so during the
  14|               Summer of 2019, great efforts were made to improve the instructional spaces, and bring in added
  15|               technology and equipment for student training purposes. In addition to new hospital/nursing home beds,
  16|               exam tables, EKG machines, geriatric manıkins, CPR manıkins, stethoscopes, blood pressure and
  17|               phlebotomy training arms, and five new testing/diagnostic machines, the school was able to purchase the
  18|               program's first-ever Virtual Dissection Technology. The high-capacity Anatomage table is something most
  19|               often found on college campuses, but has proven to be an invaluable training device to Monty Tech Health
  20|               occupations students studying for one of their many certification exams. Every Junior enrolled in the Health
  21|               Occupations program earned CPR certification; thirteen Juniors received their nursing assistant certificate
  22|               (only fourteen students took the exam prior to school closing in March 2020. Those who did not pass or
  23|               complete the CNA exam will be able to do so during the 2020-2021 school year.) Students at all levels
  24|               completed pandemic-specific training, and obtained the National COVID-Ready Caregiver Certification,
  25|               which was recommended by the Department of Public Health for all healthcare professionals. Finally,
  26|               through a unique collaboration with Mount Wachusett Community College, three motivated Seniors
  27|               completed the Emergency Medical Technician training program, earning eight college credits at no cost, and
  28|               now have a valuable career pathway available to them. (Total student enrollment: 107)
  29|               House Carpentry: The Monty Tech House Carpentry program supported a number of community
  30|               organizations during the abbreviated 2019-2020 school year, completing projects that include: building a
  31|               24'x40' lean-to roof at the Hubbardston DPW Salt Barn, construction of a Habitat for Humanity home in
  32|               Ashburnham, and finishing all interior trim at the NEADS carriage house, located in Princeton. Students
  33|               and instructors were also called upon construct a prefabricated stage for the Fitchburg State University
  34|               Theatre Club, and were delighted to work closely with our post-secondary partners in doing so. Sophomore
  35|               students handcrafted a number of beautiful Adirondack chairs, which will be used for charitable donations
  36|               in the months to come. Finally, eight Senior students earned co-op placements, and enjoyed working with
  37|               area businesses representing the carpentry and construction trades. (Total student enrollment: 54)
  38|               HVAC & Property Maintenance: Monty Tech HVAC and Property Maintenance students and instructors
  39|               participate in the home building project each year, installing the HVAC system and a number of other tasks
  40|               associated with residential construction efforts. In 2019-2020, Monty Tech HVAC students and instructors
  41|               worked in Ashburnham to support the Habitat for Humanity community service project. In addition, they
  42|               traveled to Sterling Municipal Light Department to complete work there. The Monty Tech HVAC &
  43|               Property Maintenance program also established a partnership with the Monty Tech School of Continuing
  44|               Education, to offer Oil Burner Technician training and certification to the high school students. After
  45|               earning National EPA Certification for refrigerant recovery, a total of nine Seniors were placed in area
  46|               businesses through the school's popular co-op program. HVAC Technicians, and specifically Oil Burner
  47|               Technicians, are in demand across North Central Massachusetts, so the addition of this credential will prove
  48|               to be beneficial to all program graduates who choose to stay in the HVAC and Property Maintenance trades.
  49|               While Junior students were not eligible to go out on co-op in 2019-2020, instructors offered high-quality
  50|               online instruction and support, so that they might continue to refine their technical skills and proficiency
  51|               using various online lessons and assessments. (Total student enrollment: 64)

  32|             The Cooperative Education Program is an extension of the student's technical education that combines
  33|             classroom instruction with on-the-job-training. The Co-op Program provides students with an opportunity to
  34|             further develop academic, technical and employability skills in an industry work environment. All students
  35|             are eligible to participate in the Co-op Program, provided they satisfy state and school grade, attendance and
  36|             performance requirements. All Co-op students have completed the OSHA recognized Career Safe online
  37|             health and safety course.
  38|             The Cooperative Education Program is beneficial for both student and employer. For the student, it is a
  39|             chance to gain on-the-job training that improves both their technical and employability skills. Additionally,
  40|             making industry connections enhances post-graduate employment opportunities. The Co-op Program allows
  41|             students to earn while they learn.
  42|             Employer benefits include addressing workforce needs in an efficient manner. Co-op students provide a pool of
  43|             temporary and potential full-time employees who are already trained, thus reducing employer training costs. Co-
  44|             op work hours may be tailored to suit the needs of partnering employers. Monty Tech was on pace for a record
  45|             -breaking year for co-op placements, with 145 students (Juniors and Seniors) working, learning and applying
  46|             their technical skills related in area businesses in February of 2020. Unfortunately, due to the ongoing
  47|             pandemic, co-op opportunities for our students have declined slightly, but are expected to rebound when local
  48|             businesses are able to open and operate at full capacity.

===PAGE 140=== (ocr, 49 lines)
   1|               Student Support Services
   2|               During the 2019-2020 school year, Montachusett Regional Vocational Technical School District provided
   3|               special services to approximately three hundred students - measuring progress of over two hundred students
   4|               on Individual Education Plans (IEPs) and just under one hundred students adhering to individualized
   5|               Section 504 plans. While the Student Support Services (SSS) Department encompasses special education,
   6|               the department provides support and is available to all Monty Tech students.
   7|               The department includes a full-time nursing staff that administers medications, performs state-mandated
   8|               health screening exams, and provides, when necessary, health information to the special education team for
   9|               a student's IEP meeting. The department benefits from a full-time school social worker that participates in
  10|               departmental meetings                and assists students who have needs concerning finances, family issues,
  11|               homelessness, maternity, health issues, and proper food and clothing. The school is also fortunate to have
  12|               on staff a full-time psychologist, whose role it is to evaluate all students referred for an initial evaluation or
  13|               who require a three-year re-evaluation. In addition, we have a full-time speech language pathologist, who is
  14|               available to assist students with disabilities, assess these students and consult with teachers. Our students
  15|               also have access to the services of a full-time adjustment counselor and part-time school psychologist. All
  16|               of these individuals are available for scheduled counseling sessions, mental health emergency treatment,
  17|               medical/mental health re-entry and transition support services, as well as crisis intervention.
  18|               The school's Director of Student Support Services oversees the District's Special Education Program, which
  19|               is reviewed annually in May, in accordance with regulatory requirements. The comprehensive review and
  20|                evaluation are done in collaboration with the Parent Advisory Council, and the results of the evaluation are
  21|               used to improve the special education procedures and programs                                             in place at Montachusett Regional
  22|               Vocational Technical School.
  23|               During the 2019-2020 Montachusett Regional Technical School District's Department of Student Support
  24|               Services made a concerted effort to increase social, emotional, and community based supports. In addition
  25|               to our current re-entry and school based counseling services, Monty Tech and LUK, Inc. joined forces with
  26|               the help of the Department of Public Health to provide services to at-risk students and their families.
  27|               Couple this with the further expansion of our partnership with the SHINE Initiative, it has allowed the
  28|               district to build upon our mission to provide opportunities for all students to be engaged, lead, and access
  29|               the supports necessary to improve their social and emotional well being.
  30|               Technology
  31|               The 2019-2020 school year proved to be a test in many ways for the Monty Tech educational community,
  32|               but was no match for the school's Technology Department. Because of forward thinking on the part of the
  33|               department's Director and the school's leadership team, 2019-2020 was the school's 4*h year for the 1:1
  34|               Chromebook initiative, so students and instructors had previous experience with the tools and technology
  35|               needed to learn in a remote setting. To support the additional and now required use of this technology, the
  36|               Technology Department spent countless hours during the school year upgrading the network and increasing
  37|               the bandwidth coming into the school to sustain video and audio conferencing.
  38|               Department personnel upgraded the learning experience for students, by purchasing laptops for students and
  39|               teachers, building new iMacs for the Graphic Communications program, and rebuilding surplus computers
  40|               for full remote students in CAD Drafting & Design, Graphic Communications, Business Technology, and
  41|               Engineering, so that they may work from home on their vocational assignments. A ZOOM license was
  42|               purchased, and the Instructional Technologist worked diligently to purchase and deploy software for all
  43|               teachers. Camcorders, wireless headsets, interactive displays and a variety of apps were purchased,
  44|               installed, and put into the hands of our educators to engage learners at all levels.
  45|               Finally, to ensure "business as usual," we invested in Splashtop, VPN client and configured the firewall, so
  46|               that faculty and staff laptops could be fixed remotely if a software issue presented itself, and so that the
  47|               Business Office would have remote access into the school to maintain a secure working environment, even
  48|               when the building was closed
  49|                                                                                              140
