   1|             Marine Corps JROTC
   2|             The Monty Tech Marine Corps Junior Reserve Officer Training Corps (MCJROTC) remains one of the
   3|             school's most worthwhile and engaging programs available to our students. Due to outstanding performance
   4|             throughout the year, Monty Tech's Marine Corps Junior Reserve Officers' Training Corps program was, once
   5|             again, designated as a Marine Corps Honor School. Selection for the Marine Corps Honor School means that
   6|             the school's Marine Corps JROTC program was in the top 15% of the 68 other programs in Region 1. Region
   7|             1 includes schools from the mid-Atlantic and Northeast U.S. The selection is based on several criteria
   8|            including:
   9|             • Inspector General's inspection results
  10|             • Number of cadets in the program
  11|             • Number of community service hours conducted
  12|             • Number of public affairs events attended by cadets
  13|             • Number of academic awards presented to cadets
  14|             • Participation in drill team competitions, marksmanship competitions, physical fitness competitions, and
  15|                  field trips that support the growth and development of the cadet
  16|             Because of this distinction, Monty Tech MJROTC instructors are allowed to nominate up to six cadets to each
  17|             of the three service academies — U.S. Naval Academy, U.S. Military Academy and U.S. Air Force Academy.
  18|             The 2019-2020 Corps of Cadets completed over 4,800 hours of community service in the abbreviated year,
  19|             completing a 10 mile March A Thon that raised $20,000 to support local nonprofit organizations. The Cadets
  20|             525,00 or timides in thai rely the Moing Trich Celet Cops prion dad i Color ad a for ocal that
  21|             and veteran ceremonies, a highlight for students and instructors alike.
  22|             Monty Tech School of Continuing Education
  23|             The Monty Tech School of Continuing Education continues to update and add courses that emphasize a
  24|             commitment to excellence by offering affordable, quality, and enjoyable educational experiences. For the Fall
  25|             of 2019, Monty Tech offered 142 classes and another 114 in the Spring 2020 semester.
  26|             The program is on track to support the North Central Massachusetts workforce readiness pipeline with an
  27|             increase in career and licensure courses. The pivot to a hybrid learning model has been beneficial to both our
  28|             career/licensure students as well as our personal enrichment students. During the Fall 2019 and Spring 2020
  29|             sessions, we realized the resiliency of our students and their willingness to persevere with their training despite
  30|             the challenges. Our instructors worked tirelessly to maintain training programs timelines.
  31|             Our medical students' skill set was and continues to be in high demand due to the pandemic. We were able to
  32|             support our local medical facilities with newly license healthcare workers, to include Pharmacy Technicians,
  33|             Medical Billing and Coding Specialists, EKG Technicians, Phlebotomists, CNAs, and Patient Care
  34|             Technicians.
  35|             The continued success and sustainability of an adult education program is based on hiring quality instructors and
  36|             meeting the needs of the community and supporting workforce development. Our goals in the coming year
  37|             include: identifying additional training opportunities to meet the needs of the regional workforce, provide
  38|             affordable and meaningful training opportunities to our nontraditional student population, and expanding
  39|             programming to include off-site, daytime training opportunities across the region.
  40|             Practical Nursing Program
  41|             The Monty Tech Practical Nursing Program is designed to prepare graduates to practice safely and ethically in
  42|             a caring manner for patients who are experiencing common variations in health status in diverse health care
  43|             settings.
  44|             On June 25, 2020 a graduating class of 28 students completed the Practical Nursing Program and entered the
  45|             nursing profession.
  46|                                                                                              141

===PAGE 142=== (ocr, 50 lines)
   1|               The class has thus far achieved a pass rate of 75%, with 21 of the 28 graduates passing the NCLEX-PN
   2|               exam (National Council Licensure Examination for Practical Nurses). Due to the pandemic, the testing
   3|               period has been extremely delayed. Seven graduates will pursue the examination in the coming months.
   4|               All of the 2020 graduates are currently employed in the health care profession throughout Massachusetts,
   5|               working in various health care settings, such as long-term care, sub-acute care, mental health/substance
   6|               abuse facilities, physician's offices and correctional medicine.
   7|               The Monty Tech Practical Nursing Program continues to strengthen the "LPN to BSN (Bachelor of Science
   8|               in Nursing) Bridge" relationship with Fitchburg State University. Several 2019 and 2020 graduates are
   9|               pursuing seats in the LPN to BSN program at FSU and will be continuing their education to the Bachelors in
  10|               Nursing. Many class applicants cited their attraction to Monty Tech as the opportunity to complete a ten-
  11|               month accelerated program and then bridge to Fitchburg State University. In the 6-year history of the Bridge
  12|               Program, all of Monty Tech's Practical Nursing graduates reaching graduation at FSU have passed their RN
  13|               -NCLEX exam.
  14|               Monty Tech Practical Nursing students are now completing patient scenarios in the Sim Lab on a weekly
  15|               basis in Terms 2, 3, and 4. The Faculty Sim team has implemented National League of Nursing (NLN)
  16|               patient scenarios and authored several others consistent with our curriculum frameworks, and have also
  17|               utilized the Sim lab setting to instruct students in developing nursing skills that they may not be
  18|               experiencing in the clinical setting. Instructors have successfully developed and implemented a library of
  19|               scenarios reflecting INASCAL Standards (International Association for Clinical Simulation and Learning).
  20|               In Maternity Sim Lab Boot Camp, students utilize the new simulation models to imitate the birthing
  21|               process, from obstetric office visits through labor and common post-partum complications. Well newborn
  22|               care is also taught. Student feedback has been positive regarding these new maternity scenarios, stating it
  23|               better prepares them for an unexpected outcome during a delivery. This expanded content also better
  24|               prepares students for NACE (Nursing Acceleration Challenge Exam) success at FSU.
  25|               Substance abuse and the mental health setting job opportunities have increased with more funding being
  26|               provided to combat the opiate crisis. Even without having specialty clinical rotations, students performing
  27|               simulation scenarios involving substance abuse clients, has prepared our graduates to work in a substance
  28|               abuse clinical setting, as well.
  29|                We are expecting an accreditation visit from the Massachusetts Board of Registration in the coming year.
  30|               Our goals include stabilization of faculty positions, securing a certified Simulation Instructor, and pursuance
  31|               of ACEN (Accreditation Commission for Education in Nursing) to more accurately reflect the changing
  32|               environment of nursing education.
  33|               Looking Ahead
  34|               While 2019-2020 ended in an unexpected manner, with students and instructors working and learning from
  35|               home, we were able to use that time to reflect on the core values represented within our educational
  36|               community and consider how we might improve the educational experience for all students upon their
  37|               return. We understand that the pandemic brought about many changes to the way we live and work. Our
  38|               students were isolated for weeks and months, while our instructors were asked to completely redefine their
  39|               instruction to deliver an entirely remote curriculum. People of all ages, socio-economic classes and
  40|               intellectual abilities feared for the safety of their family, friends and loved ones - compounding feelings of
  41|               loneliness and uncertainty. As we looked ahead, then, a number of priorities for the upcoming school year
  42|               emerged.
  43|               Supporting students and staff both socially and emotionally, providing the resources needed to succeed in a
  44|               remote learning environment, and maintaining a clean and safe learning environment have become our latest
  45|               priorities.
  46|               The changes brought upon by the pandemic were not expected. There was no time to prepare our staff and
  47|               students for what might lie ahead, but I am thankful for the expertise and flexibility demonstrated by our
  48|               very talented faculty and staff, and the students who made the most of a very difficult end of the 2019-2020
  49|               school year.
  50|                                                                                              142

===PAGE 143=== (ocr, 38 lines)
   1|             Programs and initiatives that we expect to implement in 2020-2021 that will have a positive impact on our
   2|             school and students are as follows:
   3|             Support faculty, staff and students' socio-emotional wellness, and develop a community-wide understanding
   4|             of equity (or lack thereof) and how it contributes to a safe and supportive learning environments for all
   5|             students. We recognize that students and staff have been home and isolated from others for months.
   6|             Learning from home may have presented an unintended result - unequal learning opportunities. There are
   7|             fears and concerns among staff, and there is a heightened sensitivity to what this isolation has meant to our
   8|             student population. Collaborating with community service agencies, we will address efforts to create a
   9|             healthy school climate, and will provide targeted training opportunities to better serve our students. A
  10|             diverse team of professionals (teachers, paraprofessionals, guidance counselors, and support staff) will review
  11|             existing district and school documents and policies, survey staff and students regarding school climate, and
  12|             develop a comprehensive action plan, and infuse those elements into the 2021-2024 District Improvement
  13|              Plan.
  14|             Expand technology resources and infrastructure to support Hybrid and/or Remote Learning Platforms.
  15|             Monty Tech is fortunate to have been able to provide all students with a Chromebook for the past several
  16|             years, and all teachers with Chromebooks or laptops to support curriculum development efforts while away
  17|             from the school. That technology proved to be invaluable during the 2019-2020 school year, and so the
  18|             District is committed to maintaining and expanding access to instructional technology that will enhance at-
  19|             home learning moving forward. During the summer of 2020, we learned that school would not resume with
  20|             all students in-person. A complex schedule (balancing academic and vocational in-person instructional time)
  21|             was negotiated and agreed upon, and students/parents were given the option of returning in a hybrid of fully
  22|             remote model. By purchasing and maintaining ZOOM licenses, increasing bandwidth, reconfiguring surplus
  23|             equipment, and continuing to work with the school's Instructional Technologist to provide a variety of
  24|             workshops to academic and vocational instructors who have come to rely of any one of the two school's
  25|             Learning Management Systems (Google Classroom or Schoology), Monty Tech will be well-positioned and
  26|             prepared to deliver instruction either in-person or remotely, at a moment's notice.
  27|             Modify facilities and infrastructure to ensure students, faculty and staff are working and learning in a safe
  28|             environment. While the school is prepared to provide all faculty and staff with required and requested PPE,
  29|             many upgrades to the school building must be completed prior to and maintained throughout the 2020-2021
  30|             school year. All rooftop units will be upgraded from MERV 8 to MERV 13 rated air filters, which are high
  31|             quality air filters typically used in general surgery or hospital settings, capturing down to 0.3 - 1.0 micron
  32|             particle size. iWave units will be added to the HVAC system, reducing pathogens, allergens, particles, smoke
  33|             and odors in the air, creating a healthy environment without producing any harmful byproducts. Installing
  34|             sanitizing stations, redirecting traffic patterns, and limiting the number of desks/seats in a confined space will
  35|             provide a comfortable setting for the students during their in-person learning days, while limiting access to
  36|             the school (parents and visitors must wait outside) will aide in screening and tracing efforts throughout the
  37|             year.
  38|                                                                                              143

===PAGE 147=== (ocr, 33 lines)
   1|                                                                                 VITALS REPORT:
   2|               AT THE RECOMMENDATION OF THE REGISTRY OF VITAL RECORDS AND U.S. DEPARTMENT OF
   3|               STATE AND FOR THE SAFETY AND SECURITY OF THE RESIDENTS OF TOWN, NAMES OF
   4|               INDIVIDUALS HAVE BEEN ELIMINATED AND INSTEAD THE NUMBER OF OCCURANCES OF EACH
   5|               BIRTH, MARRIAGE AND DEATH ARE TOTALLED FOR THE YEAR.
   6|                                              DURING 2020 THE TOWN CLERK RECORDED THE FOLLOWING:
   7|                                            BIRTH - 97                        DEATHS - 108                                 MARRIAGES - 34
   8|               *****                                                           ******                         ************************************
   9|               2020 was quite the challenge! In March we had the Presidential Primary, in addition to that election we had
  10|               a Special State Primary to fill the vacancy created by Representative Benson's resignation. Thank you
  11|               Representative Benson for your service!
  12|               Just ten days later should have been the Citizens Party Caucus - then- PANDEMIC - SHUTDOWN - Social
  13|                Distancing - Masks — Sanitizer... The Caucus was rescheduled two more times and finally dropped; only a
  14|               few candidates were able/willing to gather signatures to have their names placed on the ballot. All was not
  15|               lost, facebook to the rescue, a slate of candidates was produced (private citizens) and the list handed out
  16|               (150 feet away) to voters as they went in to the polls.
  17|               March again saw an election postponed, the Special State Election was moved to June 2nd
  18|               Special legislation was enacted to change our Annual Town Meeting and Election from May to June.
  19|               Deadlines were moved, early voting was extended to local elections (in-person and mail-in) along with the
  20|               number of days of in-person Early Voting; many thanks to United Parish for the use of their building.
  21|               The State mailed post card applications to every voter in the system for both the September Primary and the
  22|               November State (Presidential) Election; as you can see in the picture below, we were inundated with
  23|               applications for one or both of the elections and instead of three to six hundred absentee ballots we were
  24|               closer to two thousand.
  25|               Thankfully, we were able to get through it safely but not without a LOT of help from ALL town
  26|               departments; biggest thanks to the Registrars shown in photo Ruth Anderson, Shirley McBride (who, by the
  27|               way, were never doing another Presidential election again after 2016) and Karen Kemp who is our resident
  28|               sign maker etc. These ladies worked tirelessly making Early/Absent voting happen for five and ten day
  29|               stretches without a glitch! Also, couldn't have managed without the Election Workers who bravely stepped
  30|               up to cover all the polling hours of six elections but covered check-in for two Town Meetings as well -
  31|               Thank you all for a job well done!
  32|                                                                                                                      ТОШАВ ДАГОРНО
  33|                                                                                              147

===PAGE 148=== (ocr, 32 lines)
   1|                                                                       TOWN MEETING EXCERPTS
   2|                                                              ANNUAL TOWN MEETING - JUNE 13, 2020
   3|               Due to the COVID-19 pandemic, voters were checked-in outside to provide the appropriate distancing.
   4|               Several officers from the Lunenburg Police Department were on hand to assist, direct and usher attendees to
   5|               appropriate locations depending on whether they were a voter or visitor.
   6|               The Moderator called the meeting to order at 9:10 a.m. in the auditorium of the Middle/High School. The
   7|               Moderator declared that a quorum was present and the warrant had been properly served. After the Pledge
   8|               of Allegiance, the National Anthem was sung by Rose Beardmore (2020 graduate of Lunenburg High
   9|               School). Selectman Tom Alonzo recognized Phyllis Luck and Damon McQuaid for their service to the town
  10|               of Lunenburg. The Town Manager presented flowers to Mr. McQuaid. Mrs. Luck did not attend the town
  11|               meeting. The Moderator then nominated Robert Bowen as Deputy Moderator for the rest of the year. The
  12|               Moderator went over the rules for this Annual Town Meeting (in order to help keep everyone safer during
  13|               this COVID-19 pandemic):
  14|               • The cards given to voters when they arrived were to be used for all votes (no voice votes).
  15|               • Lunenburg police officers were available to escort voters to the auditorium and show apart in each open
  16|                    row were taped off to allow for social distancing) as well as the disinfectant station that was available
  17|                    for hand washing. If a registered voter chose not to wear a facemask, there was a section of the
  18|                     auditorium reserved for them to sit; this area was not used as everyone wore a face covering.
  19|                 When wishing to speak on an article, the voter needed to stand behind either of the two microphones
  20|                     available. Maintaining social distancing as noted by the markings on the floor, the voter was not to touch
  21|                    the microphone while stating their name and address prior to speaking to the article. Staff was available
  22|                    to raise or lower microphone and clean as needed.
  23|                  For point of order, seven (7) voters needed to stand up with cards raised (not calling out from seat).
  24|               • After a warrant article was read, raising your voting card would second the motion (again, no calling
  25|                    out).
  26|                  The consent calendar included articles considered non-controversial (in order to shorten the time needed
  27|                     to complete the articles for this annual town meeting.
  28|               The first order of business was the Consent Calendar which included those articles considered to be non-
  29|               controversial - the articles passed by Unanimous Consent were: #1, 2, 4, 9, 10, 11, 12, 13, 14, 15, 16, 17
  30|               and 18. Article 18 was amended to add the written report (which was given to voters when checking in) to
  31|               the Consent Calendar.
  32|                                                                                              148

  43|                                                                                              158

===PAGE 159=== (ocr, 41 lines)
   1|                                                             ANNUAL TOWN MEETING - JUNE 13, 2020
   2|             § 140-1 Purpose.
   3|             The purpose of this article and associated regulations is to regulate earth removal operations for the
   4|             protection of human health, public safety, welfare, and the Town's natural resources in the Town of
   5|             Lunenburg. Further, the provisions of this article are intended to protect abutting property owners from dust,
   6|              washouts, noise, ponds, and other hazardous land conditions that could potentially be created by poorly
   7|             managed earth removal operations.
   8|             § 140-2 Definitions.
   9|             As used in this bylaw, the following terms shall have the meanings indicated:
  10|             ABUTTERS
  11|             The owners of property within 300 feet of the perimeter of the subject property.
  12|             APPLICANT
  13|             The owner of land from which earth will be removed, or the person of lawful standing in the stead of such
  14|             owner. Any person, company, or agent standing instead of the actual property owner must have the owner's
  15|             written permission to do so.
  16|             BOARD
  17|             The Planning Board of the Town of Lunenburg, which shall act as the permit granting authority (PGA).
  18|             EARTH
  19|             This term shall include soil, loam, sand, gravel, clay, peat, rock, or other native earth material in solid form.
  20|             EARTH REMOVAL OPERATION
  21|             Any commercial mining, stripping, quarrying, digging or blasting of earth originating from the Town of
  22|             Luneburg and its transportation into or out of the Town of Lunenburg.
  23|             EROSION
  24|             The process by which the ground surface is worn by forces such as wind, running water, ice, abrasion,
  25|             gravity, transportation or by artificial means.
  26|             GRAVEL
  27|             Loose fragments of rock or coarse aggregate resulting from natural disintegration and abrasion of rock or
  28|             processing of weakly bound conglomerate.
  29|             LOAM OR TOPSOIL
  30|             A soil consisting of a friable mixture of varying proportions of clay, sand, silt, and organic matter.
  31|             PGA
  32|             The permit granting authority, which will be the Planning Board for the purposes of this article.
  33|             PROCESSING
  34|             The sorting or separation of earth materials into distinct categories based on particle size or type usually
  35|             through the use of a screening process, not including stone crushing.
  36|             SILT
  37|             Loose sedimentary material with rock particles usually less than 1/16 mm or less in diameter based on the
  38|              Wentworth scale of measurement.
  39|             A distinct portion of one or more contiguous lots, under the same or different ownerships, on which an earth
  40|             removal operation is conducted, or is proposed to be conducted, under the permit.
  41|                                                                                              159

   1|                                                               ANNUAL TOWN MEETING - JUNE 13, 2020
   2|               E. Earth removal within the right-of-way for a new subdivision road that has been approved by the
   3|                       Planning Board where there is already a bond in place with the Town to ensure performance.
   4|               F. Earth removal in conjunction with a Driveway Permit issued by the Town of Lunenburg.
   5|               The aforementioned items are exempted provided that they do not exceed any of the following thresholds:
   6|                     1) the earth removal operation is limited to no more than three contiguous lots and does not exceed a total
   7|                                 area of six acres, and
   8|                     ii) he operation is not conducted, maintained, and/or left in a condition so as to alter the natural drainage
   9|                                flow beyond the property; or to cause dust, silt, soil, or other materials to be deposited on
  10|                                adjacent properties; or to otherwise cause nuisances, hazards, or other objectionable conditions

  12|                     ill) The excavation is not active for a period of time greater than is reasonable for the activity being
  13|                                conducted.
  14|                     iv) Any earth removal is limited to the necessary scope of the project.
  15|               The PGA shall, upon petition by an abutter or by any Town Official or Town Board, review an operation that
  16|               is proceeding as exempt from the permit requirement pursuant to this § 140-4. If it is determined that the
  17|               operation does not qualify for any of the exemptions outlined in § 140-4, a permit will be required.
  18|               § 140-5 Continuation of Existing soil removal operations.
  19|               A.          An existing operation that had been issued a permit by the Board of Selectmen under the Earth
  20|                           Removal Bylaw that was in effect prior to the effective date of this Bylaw may continue to operate
  21|                           according to the terms of such permit, which shall hereafter be enforceable by the PGA pursuant to
  22|                           this article.
  23|               B. The right of an existing operation to operate pursuant to § 140-5A under a permit previously issued by
  24|                     the Board of Selectmen, or to operate without a permit pursuant to § 140-5B, shall expire if the operation
  25|                     is discontinued. An operation shall be deemed to have been discontinued if no earth removal activity
  26|                     occurs at the site for a period of 12 consecutive months.
  27|               § 140-6 Permit criteria for earth removal operations.
  28|               A. Permits for earth removal operations shall be granted by the PGA only upon its written determination
  29|                     that the proposed use shall not cause substantial detriment to the neighborhood, or the Town, taking into
  30|                     account the characteristics of the site and the proposal in relation to the site and surrounding
  31|                     environment. In addition to any specific factors that may be set forth elsewhere in this article and its
  32|                     associated regulations adopted pursuant to § 140-8, such determination shall include consideration of
  33|                     each of the following:
  34|               (1) Social, economic, or community needs which are served by the proposal;
  35|               (2) Impact of the Earth Removal Operation on traffic flow and safety, including loading and unloading;
  36|               (3) Neighborhood character and the effect of the proposed Earth Removal Operation on that character;
  37|               (4) Impacts on the natural environment, including any proposed remediation for Earth Removal Activities;
  38|               (5) Potential fiscal impact, including impact on Town services, tax base and property values.
  39|                                                                                              161

===PAGE 162=== (ocr, 41 lines)
   1|                                                               ANNUAL TOWN MEETING - JUNE 13, 2020
   2|               B. Determination for each of the five criteria shall be set forth in the permit decision as findings of fact.
   3|               § 140-7 General limitations.
   4|               A. No permit shall be issued for the removal of earth in any location if such an operation:
   5|               (1) Will endanger the public safety, public health or constitute a nuisance.
   6|               (2) Will produce noise, dust, or other noxious effects observable at the lot lines of the property in amounts
   7|                     objectionable or detrimental to the normal use of adjacent properties.
   8|               (3) Will result in the transportation of materials in such manner as to cause traffic congestion, dust,
   9|                       spillage, noise, or other nuisances or hazards, particularly on residential streets.
  10|               (4) Will result in the transportation over ways which will be unduly injured thereby.
  11|               (5) Will result in a change of topography and cover which will be disadvantageous to the most appropriate
  12|                       final use of the land or to the use of lands adjacent to the site.
  13|               (6) Will cause irreparable harm to or loss of important wildlife, wildlife habitat or rare plant species
  14|                       indigenous to the area.
  15|               (7) Will result in stormwater damaging abutting properties.
  16|               B. Applicants are encouraged to submit a written statement regarding how each of the previous items will
  17|                     be addressed and/or mitigated.
  18|               § 140-8 Regulations.
  19|               The PGA may adopt and periodically amend rules and regulations for the implementation of this article, by
  20|               majority vote. Such rules and regulations may set forth performance standards for earth removal operations,
  21|               impose filing and consultant fees, define additional terms not inconsistent with the article, and establish
  22|               administrative procedures. Proposed Rules and Regulations shall be adopted by the PGA at a public hearing.
  23|               Notice of the hearing shall be published by the PGA in a newspaper of general circulation in each of two
  24|               successive weeks, the first publication being not less than 14 days before the day of said hearing. Failure by
  25|               the PGA to adopt such rules and regulations or a legal declaration of their invalidity by a court of law shall
  26|               not act to suspend or invalidate the effect of this article.
  27|               § 140-9 Permit procedures.
  28|               A. Any person wishing to obtain a permit to remove material on a property in the Town, or to use any
  29|                     public way within the Town for transporting such material, shall file a completed application for a
  30|                     permit together with any required supporting data and maps with the PGA and the filing fee, as
  31|                     established in the regulations of the PGA.
  32|               B. Any permit issued is nontransferable and shall automatically expire upon completion of the earth
  33|                     removal project for which it was issued or at such time as may be specified in said permit. In no case
  34|                     shall a permit be issued initially for a period longer than one year. A permit may be renewed for up to
  35|                     two years or lesser time as determined by the PGA after evidence is presented that all conditions of the
  36|                     expiring permit have been complied with and the work has been performed in good faith. There is no
  37|                     limit on the number of permit renewals an applicant can apply for. A public hearing may be required by
  38|                     the PGA, at its discretion, for renewal of permits.
  39|               C. Upon approval of all permits will expire on the following June 30. Thereafter all renewals will be for a
  40|                           period of 1 or 2 years running from July 1 to June 30.
  41|                                                                                              162

===PAGE 163=== (ocr, 43 lines)
   1|                                                              ANNUAL TOWN MEETING - JUNE 13, 2020
   2|             § 140-10 Public hearing.
   3|              A. Once a complete permit application is filed with the PGA, the PGA shall set a date for a public hearing
   4|                   and so notify the applicant. Notice of the hearing shall be published by the PGA, at the applicant's
   5|                   expense, in a newspaper of general circulation in each of two successive weeks, the first publication being
   6|                   not less than 14 days before the day of said hearing. Notice of the hearing shall be given to all owners of
   7|                   real estate abutting upon the land specified in the permit application or lying within 300 feet of the subject
   8|                   parcel, all as appearing on the most recent tax list. The PGA will arrange for the publication and
   9|                   transmission of the notice of the hearing to the abutters, and the costs will be billed to the applicant. Final
  10|                   approval for the permit shall not be made until all hearing fees have been paid in full.
  11|             B. The PGA's final action, rendered in writing, shall consist of either:
  12|                   (1) Approval of the application based upon determination that the proposed plan is in compliance with the
  13|                        standards set forth in this Bylaw; or
  14|                   (2). Disapproval of the application based upon a determination that the proposed plan does not meet the
  15|                          standards set forth in this Bylaw; or
  16|                   (3) Approval of the application subject to any condition, modification or restriction required by the
  17|                        Planning Board which will ensure that the project meets the standards set forth in this Bylaw.
  18|             § 140-11 Bond or security.
  19|             The Board may require suitable bond or other security adequate to assure compliance with any of the
  20|             provisions of this bylaw.
  21|             $ 140-12 Enforcement; violations and penalties.
  22|              A.         The PGA or its authorized agent shall enforce this article, its regulations, and the requirements and
  23|                         conditions of permits issued thereunder and may pursue civil and criminal remedies for violations of
  24|                        the same.
  25|             B.         The Building Inspector is authorized to conduct inspections on behalf of the PGA. To the extent
  26|                        permitted by law, or if authorized by the owner, operator, or other person in control of the site, the
  27|                        Building Inspector or other agent of the PGA may enter on the site to conduct inspections.
  28|             C.          The Building Inspector or other authorized agent of the PGA may issue such orders as are deemed
  29|                         necessary to stop violations and ensure compliance with this article, its regulations, and permits,
  30|                        including an order to cease and desist operations. Such orders may be issued by certified mail, return
  31|                        receipt requested, or by delivery, to the property owner, permit holder, or person responsible for
  32|                        operations at the site.
  33|             D.         The PGA may seek injunctive relief to restrain violations or to compel abatement or
  34|                         remediation of violations.
  35|             E. Any person who violates any provision of this article or regulations, permits, and orders issued
  36|                        thereunder may be punished by a fine of not more than $300 per offense. Each day that such violation
  37|                        occurs or continues shall constitute a separate offense.
  38|             F.         As an alternative to a criminal fine, the Town may utilize the noncriminal disposition procedures of
  39|                        MGL c. 40, § 21D, in which case the Building Inspector shall be the enforcing person. The penalty for
  40|                        the first offense shall be $50. The penalty for the second offense shall be $100. The penalty for the
  41|                        third and subsequent offenses shall be $200.
  42|             G. If the violator holds a permit issued under this article, the PGA may revoke the permit after a public
  43|                                                                                              163

===PAGE 164=== (ocr, 40 lines)
   1|                                                                ANNUAL TOWN MEETING - JUNE 13, 2020
   2|               hearing, at which point all operations shall cease until such time as the necessary measures are taken to assure
   3|               compliance with this article and a new permit is issued.
   4|               § 140-13 Severability.
   5|               The provisions of this article are severable, and the invalidity of any section, subdivision, paragraph, or other
   6|               part of this article shall not affect the validity or effectiveness of the remainder of the article.
   7|               §140-14 Road Openings
   8|               A.         Permit Required; liability for cost:

  26|               action relative thereto. (Submitted by the Planning Board)
  27|                                                                              Chapter 140
  28|                                                           Excavations and Earth Removal
  29|               [HISTORY: Adopted by the Town of Lunenburg as indicated in article histories. Amendments noted
  30|               where applicable.
  31|               GENERAL REFERENCES
  32|               Streets and sidewalks — See Ch. 205.
  33|               Zoning — See Ch. 250.
  34|               Subdivision regulations — See Ch. 325.
  35|                                                                                Article I
  36|                                                                            Earth Removal
  37|               [Adopted 5-12-1990 (Art. IX, § 9, of the Town Bylaws)|
  38|               § 140-1 Definitions.
  39|               As used in this bylaw, the following terms shall have the meanings indicated:
  40|                                                                                              164

===PAGE 165=== (ocr, 40 lines)
   1|                                                               ANNUAL TOWN MEETING - JUNE 13, 2020
   2|               BOARD
   3|                       The Planning Board of the Town of Lunenburg.
   4|               EARTH
   5|                       Includes soil, loam, sand, gravel, and stone.
   6|               § 140-2 Purpose.
   7|               The purpose of this bylaw is to protect the health, safety and welfare of the public by regulating the removal
   8|               of earth so as not to create dust, washouts, noise, ponds, and other hazardous land conditions.
   9|               § 140-3 Enforcement; violations and penalties.
  10|               Any person, firm or corporation willfully violating, disobeying or refusing to comply with any of the
  11|               provisions of this bylaw shall be prosecuted under the terms of MGL c. 40, § 21, para. 17, and shall be
  12|               subject to a fine of not more than $50 for the first offense, not more than $100 for the second offense, and
  13|               not more than $200 for any subsequent offense. Each day during any portion of which such violation is
  14|               allowed to continue shall be considered a separate offense. The Board may revoke or suspend the permit of
  15|               any person, firm or corporation holding a permit under this bylaw if such person, firm or corporation
  16|               violates, disobeys, or fails to comply with any of its provisions.
  17|               § 140-4 Existing soil removal operations.
  18|               Any person, firm or corporation engaged in the operation of soil removal on the effective date of this bylaw
  19|               may continue such operation for 30 days after such date, but shall, within said 30 days, apply for a permit in
  20|               accordance with this bylaw. At the end of such 30 days, the operation shall be governed by the terms of the
  21|               permit issued or, if no such permit is issued, shall cease.
  22|               § 140-5 Severability.
  23|               The invalidity of any section of this bylaw shall not invalidate any other section or provision thereof.
  24|               § 140-6 Permit required.
  25|               No earth in an amount in excess of 10 cubic yards shall be moved from any parcel of land within the Town
  26|               of Lunenburg to another parcel either within or without the Town unless such removal is authorized by a
  27|               permit issued by the Board. No earth in an amount in excess of 10 cubic yards shall be moved in less than
  28|               ten-cubic-yard quantities over a period of time, deemed by the Board to be for the purpose of evading any of
  29|               the provisions of this bylaw. No earth removal permit shall be issued until an application therefor is filed
  30|               with the Board and the Board has held a public hearing on the application, after publication of the time and
  31|               place, and subject matter sufficient for identification, in a newspaper having general distribution in the Town
  32|               once in each of two successive weeks, the first publication to be not less than 14 days prior to said public
  33|               hearing, and notice of such hearing shall be sent by mail to owners of abutting property prior to said public
  34|               hearing.
  35|               § 140-7 Application for permit.
  36|               Applications for such permits shall be accompanied by exhibits and documentation deemed necessary by the
  37|               Board for the proper issuance of a permit, which shall include the following:
  38|               A. Name and address of the legal owner of the land in question.
  39|               B. Name and address of petitioner, if different, and names and addresses of owners of abutting property.
  40|                                                                                              165

===PAGE 166=== (ocr, 40 lines)
   1|                                                               ANNUAL TOWN MEETING - JUNE 13, 2020
   2|                    An adequate and sufficient plan of land prepared by a registered engineer or land surveyor and
   3|                       indicating tract boundaries, adjacent streets and roads, the limits of the proposed excavation, the
   4|                       locations of all structures within 200 feet of said limits, original topography of five-foot contours,
   5|                       proposed final contours of five-foot intervals, and the location and proposed use of all structures and
   6|                       buildings to be used in connection with the removal operation. Any of the above provisions may be
   7|                       waived at discretion of the Board.
   8|               D. Statement of plans for the disposal of rock, tree stumps and other waste material, and for the drainage of
   9|                       the site and excavation during and after the removal operation.
  10|               E. Proposed closure plan as provided in § 140-13, unless waived at the discretion of the Board.
  11|               § 140-8 Denial of permit.
  12|               No permit for earth removal shall be issued if such removal will:
  13|               A. Endanger the general health, safety, or welfare or constitute a public nuisance.
  14|               B. Result in detriment to the normal use of adjacent property by reason of noise, dust, erosion, or vibration.
  15|               C. Result in traffic hazard in residential areas, or congestion and physical damage to public ways.
  16|               § 140-9 Duration of permit.
  17|                No permit for any earth removal shall be issued for more than one year's duration, and may be renewed
  18|               thereafter.
  19|               § 140-10 Control of operation.
  20|               In approving the issuance of a permit, the Board shall impose all reasonable requirements which shall be
  21|               deemed necessary by the Board and may include: grading, seeding, and planting, fencing necessary for
  22|               public safety and screening unsightly operations, methods of removal, location and use of structures, hours
  23|               of operation, routes of transportation of material removed, control of drainage, control of dust, disposition of
  24|               waste material incident to the operation, due consideration for the preservation of the Town's natural
  25|               resources, including inland swamps and waters and the aesthetic values, and final appearance of the property
  26|               on completion of the operation.
  27|               § 140-11 Bond or security.
  28|               The Board may require suitable bond or other security adequate to assure compliance with any of the
  29|               provisions of this bylaw.
  30|               § 140-12 Exemptions.
  31|               [Amended 1-10-1968]
  32|               This bylaw shall not be construed to apply to any such removal incidental to the construction or alteration of
  33|               any structure for which a building permit has been issued, or to the removal incidental to the installation of
  34|               any cesspool or septic tank for which a permit has been issued by the Board of Health, or to the grading or
  35|               development of any approved subdivision or public way.
  36|               § 140-13 Completion and closure.
  37|               [Amended 5-30-1986]
  38|               A. The Board may require a closure plan before issuance of a permit, expiration or termination of an
  39|                       existing permit.
  40|                                                                                              166

===PAGE 167=== (ocr, 35 lines)
   1|                                                               ANNUAL TOWN MEETING - JUNE 13, 2020
   2|               B. The closure plan must be approved and completed to the satisfaction of the Board before any:
   3|               (1) Building/Construction.
   4|               (2) Transfer of ownership.
   5|               (3) Termination of bond or security.
   6|                C. The closure plan shall include:
   7|               (1) Proposed final contours.
   8|               (2) Proposed final drainage.
   9|               (3) Proposed final revegetation.
  10|               (4) A drawing, drawn to scale, showing the location of any material (rock, tree stumps or any other waste

  12|               § 140-14 Permit fees.
  13|               Fees shall be established from time to time by the Planning Board. Failure to pay the earth removal permit
  14|               renewal fee shall result in the automatic nonrenewal of the earth removal permit.
  15|                                                                                          Article II

  17|               [Adopted as Art. IX, § 10, of the Town Bylaws]
  18|               § 140-15 Barriers required.
  19|               Owners of land which has been excavated shall erect barriers or take other suitable measures within five
  20|               days after such owners have been notified in writing by the Planning Board that, in their opinion, such
  21|               excavation constitutes a hazard to the public safety.
  22|               § 140-16 Violations and penalties.
  23|               The penalty for violation of this bylaw shall be as follows:
  24|               A. For the first offense: $50.
  25|                B. For the second offense: $100.
  26|               C. For each subsequent offense: $200.

  38|               ARTICLE 20: To see if the Town will vote to amend the Code of Lunenburg, Chapter 204-1, entitled
  39|               "Stormwater Management; NPDES Phase II Permits," as depicted below, with additions underlined and
  40|               deletions stricken-through, or take any other action relative thereto. (Submitted by the Planning Board))
  41|               (Select Board recommend approval; Finance Committee - No Financial Impact) VOTED to amend the
  42|               Code of Lunenburg, Chapter 204-1, entitled "Stormwater Management; NPDES Phase II Permits," as
  43|               depicted below, with additions underlined and deletions stricken-through.
  44|               § 204-1 Stormwater management; NDES Phase II permits.
  45|                A. Purpose and intent.
  46|                                                                                              171

===PAGE 172=== (ocr, 45 lines)
   1|                                                            SPECIAL TOWN MEETING - November 17, 2020
   2|             (1) Regulation of discharges to the municipal separate storm sewer system (MS4) is necessary for the
   3|                    protection of the Town of Lunenburg's water bodies and groundwater, and to safeguard the public health,
   4|                    safety, welfare and the environment. Increased and contaminated stormwater runoff associated with
   5|                    developed land uses and the accompanying increase in impervious surface are major causes of
   6|                    impairment of water quality and flow in lakes, ponds, streams, rivers, wetlands, and groundwater which
   7|                    result in the contamination of drinking water supplies; erosion of stream channels; alteration or
   8|                    destruction of aquatic and wildlife habitat; and flooding. This bylaw establishes minimum stormwater
   9|                    management standards for the final conditions that result from development and redevelopment projects
  10|                    to minimize adverse impacts off-site and downstream which would be borne by abutters, townspeople
  11|                    and the general public.
  12|             (2) This regulation requires local review and approval of a stormwater management plan for all development
  13|                    and redevelopment projects that disturb one acre or more. In addition to these regulations, the owner and/
  14|                    or developer is also obligated to meet the requirements of the Federal Environmental Protection Agency's
  15|                    (EPA) regulations for stormwater management.
  16|             B. The following definitions shall apply in the interpretation and implementation of this Bylaw. Additional
  17|             definitions may be adopted by separate regulation:
  18|                    (1) ACRE: forty-three thousand, five-hundred and sixty square feet (43,560 sq. ft.)
  19|                    (2) AGRICULTURAL USE: The normal maintenance or improvement of land in agricultural or
  20|                            aquacultural use, as defined by the Massachusetts Wetlands Protection Act, M.G.L. c. 131, 8 40, and
  21|                            its implementing regulations.
  22|                    (3) ALTER: Any activity, which will measurably change the ability of a ground surface area to absorb
  23|                            water or will change existing surface drainage patterns. Alter may be similarly represented as
  24|                            "alteration of drainage characteristics," and "conducting land disturbance activities."
  25|                    (4) APPLICANT: Any person, individual, partnership, association, firm, company, corporation, trust,
  26|                            authority, agency, department, or political subdivision, of the Commonwealth of Massachusetts or
  27|                            the federal government to the extent permitted by law requesting a Stormwater Management Permit
  28|                            for proposed land-disturbance activity.
  29|                    (5) BEST MANAGEMENT PRACTICE (BMP): Structural, non-structural and managerial techniques
  30|                            that are recognized to be the most effective and practical means to prevent and/or reduce increases in
  31|                            stormwater volumes and flows, reduce point source and nonpoint source pollution, and promote
  32|                            stormwater quality and protection of the environment. "Structural" BMPs are devices that are
  33|                            engineered and constructed to provide temporary storage and treatment of stormwater runoff.
  34|                            "Nonstructural" BMPs use natural measures to reduce pollution levels, do not require extensive
  35|                            construction efforts, and/or promote pollutant reduction by eliminating the pollutant source.
  36|                    (6) BETTER SITE DESIGN: Site design approaches and techniques that can reduce a site's impact on
  37|                            the watershed through the use of nonstructural stormwater management practices. Better site design
  38|                            includes conserving and protecting natural areas and greenspace, reducing impervious cover, and
  39|                            using natural features for stormwater management.
  40|                    (7) CERTIFICATE OF COMPLETION: Document issued by the Town of Lunenburg Planning Board,
  41|                          its employees, or authorized agents upon receipt of a final inspection report and acknowledgement
  42|                          that all conditions of the Stormwater Management Permit have been satisfactorily completed.
  43|                    (8) ENFORCEMENT ORDER: A written order issued by the Town of Lunenburg to enforce the
  44|                            provisions of this Article.
  45|                                                                                              172

  13|                          (or activities that are part of a larger common plan of development disturbing greater than 1 acre
  14|                          that does not meet the definition of Redevelopment.

  16|                            snowmelt moving over and through the ground. As the runoff moves, it picks up and carries away
  17|                            natural and human-made pollutants, finally depositing them into water resource areas.
  18|                     (13) OWNER: A person with a legal or equitable interest in property.
  19|                    (14) PERSON: Any individual, group of individuals, association, partnership, corporation, company,
  20|                            business organization, trust, estate, the Commonwealth or political subdivision thereof to the extent
  21|                            subject to Town Bylaws, administrative agency, public or quasi-public corporation or body, the

  24|                            a tract of land are submitted to the Lunenburg Conservation Commission or Planning Board.
  25|                            Where phased development or plan approval occurs (preliminary grading, roads and utilities, etc.).
  26|                            the existing conditions at the time prior to the first plan submission shall establish predevelopment
  27|                            conditions.
  28|                     (16) POST-DEVELOPMENT: The conditions that reasonably may be expected or anticipated to exist
  29|                            after completion of the land development activity on a specific site or tract of land. Post-
  30|                            development refers to the phase of a new development or redevelopment project after completion,
  31|                            and does not refer to the construction phase of a project.
  32|                     (17) RECHARGE: The replenishment of underground water reserves.

  34|                            disturb the ground surface or increase the impervious area on previously developed sites. Any

   2|                     (19) STORMWATER AUTHORITY: The Town of Lunenburg Planning Board or its authorized agent
   3|                             (s). The Planning Board is responsible for coordinating the review, approval and permit process as
   4|                             detined in this Bylaw.
   5|                     (20)STORMWATER MANAGEMENT PERMIT (SMP): A permit issued by the Planning Board after
   6|                           review of an application, plans, calculations, and other supporting documents, which is designed to
   7|                           protect the environment of the Town from the deleterious effects of uncontrolled and untreated
   8|                           stormwater runoff.
   9|                     (21) WATERS OF THE COMMONWEALTH: All waters within the jurisdiction of the Commonwealth
  10|                           of Massachusetts, including, without limitation, rivers, streams, lakes, ponds, springs, impoundments,
  11|                           estuaries, wetlands, coastal waters, groundwaters, and vernal pools.
  12|                     (22)WETLAND RESOURCE AREAS: Areas specified in the Massachusetts Wetlands Protection Act
  13|                           Regulations, 310 CMR 10.00, as amended, and in the Town of Lunenburg Article XXI General
  14|                           Wetlands Protection Bylaw, as amended.
  15|              B. C The objectives of this bylaw are:
  16|              (1) To require practices to control the flow of stormwater from new and redeveloped sites to the Town's
  17|                     storm drainage system in order to prevent flooding and erosion;
  18|              (2) To protect groundwater and surface water from degradation;
  19|              (3) To promote groundwater recharge;
  20|              (4) To prevent pollutants from entering the Town's municipal separate storm sewer system (MS4) and to
  21|                     minimize discharge of pollutants from the MS4;
  22|              (5) To ensure adequate long-term operation and maintenance of structural stormwater best management
  23|                     practices so that they work as designed;
  24|              (6) To comply with state and federal statutes and regulations relating to stormwater discharges; and
  25|             (7) To establish Lunenburg's legal authority to ensure compliance with the provisions of this bylaw through
  26|                     inspection, monitoring, and enforcement.
  27|              (8) Establish regulations of land development activities that preserve the health of water resources.
  28|              (9) Require that the amount and quality of stormwater runoff from new development is equal to or better than
  29|                         pre-development conditions in order to reduce flooding, stream erosion, pollution, property damage
  30|                         and harm to aquatic life.
  31|              (10) Establish stormwater management standards and design criteria to control the quality and quantity of
  32|                         stormwater runoff.
  33|              (11) Encourage the use of low-impact development practices, such as reducing impervious cover and
  34|                         preserving greenspace and other natural areas.
  35|                                                                                              174

===PAGE 175=== (ocr, 41 lines)
   1|                                                             SPECIAL TOWN MEETING - November 17, 2020
   2|              € D. Applicability. No person may undertake a construction activity following the effective date of this
   3|                      Bylaw, including clearing, grading and excavation that results in a land disturbance that will disturb
   4|                      equal to or greater than one acre of land or will disturb less than one acre of land but part of a larger
   5|                      common plan of development or sale that will ultimately disturb equal to or greater than one acre of
   6|                      land draining to the town of Lunenburg's municipal separate storm sewer system, without a permit
   7|                      from the speciat permit granting authority. Construction activity does not include routine maintenance
   8|                      that is performed to maintain the original line and grade, hydraulic capacity or the original purpose of
   9|                      the site. Construction activities that are exempt are:
  10|              (1) Normal maintenance and improvement of land in agricultural use as defined by the Wetlands Protection
  11|                      Act regulation, 310 CMR 10.04;
  12|              (2) Maintenance of existing landscaping, gardens or lawn areas associated with a single-family dwelling;
  13|              (3) The construction of fencing that will not substantially alter existing terrain or drainage patterns;
  14|              (4) Construction of utilities other than drainage (gas, water, electric, telephone, etc.) which will not alter
  15|                      terrain or drainage patterns;
  16|              (5) Work activities of municipal, state or federal agencies or their agents outside of the Phase II boundaries
  17|                      as shown on the latest U.S. Environmental Protection Agency's National Pollutant Discharge
  18|                      Elimination System (NPDES) Phase II maps.Emergency repairs to any stormwater management facility
  19|                      or practice that poses a threat to public health or safety.
  20|              (6) As authorized in the Phase I Small MS4 General Permit for Massachusetts, stormwater discharges
  21|                      resulting from the activities identified in Subsection C(1) that are wholly subject to jurisdiction under
  22|                      the Wetlands Protection Act and demonstrate compliance with the Massachusetts Stormwater
  23|                      Management Policy as reflected in an order of conditions issued by the Conservation Commission are
  24|                      exempt from compliance with this bylaw.
  25|              Đ.E. Permits and procedures.
  26|              (1) The special permit granting authority (SPGA) under this bylaw shall be the Lunenburg Planning Board
  27|                      Such special permit shall be granted if the SPGA determines, in conjunction with the Conservation
  28|                      Commission, Department of Public Works, Board of Health and Building Commissioner, that the intent
  29|                      of this bylaw, as well as specific criteria, are met. The SPGA shall not grant a special permit under this
  30|                      section unless the petitioner's application materials include, in the SPA's opinion, sufficiently detailed,
  31|                      definite, and credible information to support positive findings in relation to the standards given in this
  32|                      section. The SPGA shall document the basis for any departures from the recommendations of the other
  33|                       Town boards or departments in its decision.
  34|              (2) The site owner or his agent shall file with the SPGA 130 copies of a completed application package for a
  35|                    stormwater management permit (SMP). Permit issuance is required prior to any site-altering activity.
  36|                    While the applicant can be a representative, the permittee must be the owner of the site. The SMP
  37|                    application package shall include:

  40|                      Subsection E(1)F of this Bylaw;

  14|                       current edition of the Department of Environmental Protection guidelines and policies. The plan shall
  15|                      be designed to also meet the policy standards of the SPGA. The stormwater management plan shall
  16|                       fully describe the project in drawings, and narrative. It shall include, at a minimum:
  17|               (a)     Names, addresses, and telephone numbers of the owner, applicant, and person(s) or firm(s) preparing

  25|                       spot grades as needed to depict detailed drainage patternstwo-foot intervals;

  29|             (2) Standards. Projects shall meet the standards of the Massachusetts Stormwater Management Policy, which
  30|             are as follows:
  31|                                                                                              178

===PAGE 179=== (ocr, 36 lines)
   1|                                                           SPECIAL TOWN MEETING - November 17, 2020
   2|               (a) No new stormwater conveyances (e.g., outfalls) may discharge untreated stormwater directly to or
   3|                     cause erosion in wetlands or water of the commonwealth;
   4|               (b) Low Impact Development (LID) site planning and design strategies must be implemented if
   5|               practicable in order to reduce the discharge of stormwater from development sites;
   6|               (c) Stormwater management system design shall be consistent with the intent and guidance provided by
   7|               the latest Massachusetts Stormwater Handbook and per industry standard methods. While design should
   8|               be completed per Massachusetts Stormwater Handbook specifications to the fullest extent feasible, the
   9|               PGA reserves the right to waive and/or modify minor disparities between the design and the
  10|               Massachusetts Stormwater Handbook on a project-specific basis.
  11|               (d) Stormwater management systems on new development shall be designed to meet an average annual
  12|               pollutant removal equivalent to 90% of the average annual load of Total Suspended Solids (TSS) related
  13|               to the total post-construction impervious area on the site AND 60% of the average annual load of Total
  14|               Phosphorus (TP) related to the total post-construction impervious surface area on the site.
  15|                                      [1] Average annual pollutant removal requirements in E.2.(d) are achieved through one of
  16|                                             the following methods:
  17|                                                   Installing BMPs that meet the pollutant removal percentages based on
  18|                                                     calculations developed consistent with EPA Region I's BMP Accounting and
  19|                                                     Tracking Tool (2016) or other BMP performance evaluation tool provided by
  20|                                                     EPA Region 1, where available. If EPA Region 1 tools do not address the
  21|                                                     planned or installed BMP performance, then any federally or State-approved
  22|                                                     BMP design guidance or performance standards (e.g., State stormwater
  23|                                                     handbooks and design guidance manuals) may be used to calculate BMP
  24|                                                     performance; or
  25|                                             (ii) Retaining the volume of runoff equivalent to, or greater than, 1.0 inch multiplied
  26|                                                    by the total post-construction impervious surface area on the new development
  27|                                                     site; or
  28|                                             (iii) Meeting a combination of retention and treatment that achieves the above
  29|                                                     standards; or
  30|                                             (iv) Utilizing offsite mitigation that meets the above standards within the same USGS
  31|                                                     HUC12 as the new development site.
  32|               (e) Stormwater management systems on redevelopment sites shall be designed to meet an average annual
  33|               pollutant removal equivalent to 80% of the average annual post-construction load of TSS related to the
  34|               total post-construction impervious area on the site AND 50% of the average annual load of TP related to
  35|               the total post-construction impervious surface area on the site.

   5|                                                   developed consistent with EPA Region 1's BMP Accounting and Tracking Tool

  24|             (3) Project changes. The permittee, or its agent, shall notify the PGA in writing of any change or alteration of
  25|                   a land-disturbing activity authorized in a stormwater management permit before any change or alteration
  26|                   occurs. If the PGA determines that the change or alteration is significant, based on the design standards
  27|                  listed in Subsection F(2) and accepted construction practices, the PGA may require that an amended
  28|                   stormwater management permit application be filed and a public hearing held. If any change or deviation
  29|                   from the stormwater management permit occurs during a project, the PGA may require the installation of
  30|                  interim measures before approving the change.
  31|             No new stormwater conveyances (e.g., outfalls) may discharge untreated stormwater directly to or cause
  32|                    erosion in wetlands or water of the commonwealth.
  33|             (b) Stormwater management systems must be designed so that post development peak discharges rates do
  34|                    not exceed pre-development peak discharge rates.
  35|             (c) Loss of annual recharge to groundwater should be minimized through the use of infiltration measures to
  36|                    the maximum extent practicable. The annual recharge from the post development site should approximate
  37|                    the annual recharge rate from the pre-development or existing site conditions, based on soil types.
  38|             (d) For new development, stormwater management systems must be designed to remove 80% of the average
  39|                    annual load (post development conditions) of total suspended solids (TSS). It is presumed that this
  40|                    standard is met when:
  41|             [1] Suitable nonstructural practices for source control and pollution prevention are implemented;
  42|                                                                                              180

===PAGE 181=== (ocr, 39 lines)
   1|                                                              SPECIAL TOWN MEETING - November 17, 2020
   2|              [2] Stormwater management best management practices (BMPs) are sized to capture the prescribed runoff
   3|                      volume; and
   4|              [3] Stormwater management BMPs are maintained as designed.
   5|              (e) Stormwater discharges from areas with higher potential pollutant loads require the use of specific
   6|                      stormwater management BMPs. The use of infiltration practices without pretreatment is prohibited.
   7|              (f) Stormwater discharges to critical areas must utilize certain stormwater management BMPs approved for
   8|                      critical areas (see MA DEP's Stormwater Management Volume I: Stormwater Policy Handbook).
   9|                      Critical areas are outstanding resource waters (ORWs), cold-water fisheries, and recharge areas for
  10|                      public water supplies.
  11|              (g) Redevelopment of previously developed sites must meet the stormwater management standards to the
  12|                      maximum extent practicable. However, if it is not practicable to meet all the standards, new (retrofitted
  13|                      or expanded) stormwater management systems must be designed to improve existing conditions.
  14|              (h) Erosion and sediment controls must be implemented to prevent impacts during disturbance and
  15|                      construction activities.
  16|              (i) All stormwater management systems must have an operation and maintenance plan to ensure that
  17|                      systems function as designed.
  18|              (j)   When one or more of the standards cannot be met, an applicant may demonstrate that an equivalent
  19|                      level of environmental protection will be provided.
  20|              (3) Project changes. The permittee, or its agent, shall notify the SPGA in writing of any change or alteration
  21|                    of a land disturbing activity authorized in a stormwater management permit before any change of
  22|                    alteration occurs. If the SPGA determines that the change or alteration is significant, based on the design
  23|                    standards listed in Subsection E(2) and accepted construction practices, the SPGA may require that an
  24|                    amended stormwater management permit application be filed and a public hearing held. If any change of
  25|                    deviation from the stormwater management permit occurs during a project, the SPGA may require the
  26|                    installation of interim measures before approving the change.
  27|              (G.) Erosion and sediment control plans. An erosion and sediment control plan is required at the time of
  28|                      application for all projects. The erosion and sediment control plan shall contain sufficient information
  29|                      about the nature and purpose of the proposed development, pertinent conditions of the site and adjacent
  30|                      areas, proposed erosion and sedimentation controls, and proposed control for other wastes on
  31|                      construction sites such as demolition debris, litter, and sanitary wastes to ensure they are not discharged
  32|                      to the MS4, drainage system, or waters of the commonwealth. The applicant shall submit such material
  33|                      as is necessary to show that the proposed development will comply with the design requirements as
  34|                      follows:
  35|                      (1) Minimize total area of disturbance.
  36|                      (2) Sequence activities to minimize simultaneous areas of disturbance.
  37|                      (3) Minimize soil erosion and control sedimentation during construction, provided that prevention of
  38|                              erosion is preferred over sedimentation control.

   1|                                                            SPECIAL TOWN MEETING - November 17, 2020
   2|                     (4) Divert uncontaminated water around disturbed areas.
   3|                    (5) Maximize infiltration and groundwater recharge.
   4|                    (6) Install, inspect, and maintain all Erosion and Sediment Control measures in accordance with the
   5|                            manufacturer's specifications and good engineering practices.
   6|                     (7) Prevent off-site transport of sediment and wastes.
   7|                    (8) Protect all storm drain inlets and armor all newly constructed outlets.
   8|                    (9) Protect and manage on and off-site material storage areas (overburden and stockpiles of dirt, borrow
   9|                            areas, or other areas used solely by the permitted project are considered a part of the project).
  10|                     (10) Comply with applicable federal, state and local laws and regulations including waste disposal,
  11|                            sanitary sewer or septic system regulations, and air quality requirements, including dust control.
  12|                     (11) Institute interim and permanent stabilization measures, which shall be instituted on a disturbed area
  13|                            as soon as practicable but no more than fourteen (14) days after construction activity has
  14|                            temporarily or permanently ceased on that portion of the site.
  15|                     (12) Properly manage on-site construction waste and materials.
  16|                     (13) Stabilize construction site entrances and exits and prevent off-site vehicle tracking of sediments.
  17|                     (14)Ensure that any stormwater BMP (for post-construction stormwater management) installed during
  18|                           construction will be protected from compaction, siltation, and erosion or will be restored or replaced
